Fetal Valproate Syndrome

Environmental MONDO:0012275 Pathograph 57 Show in embeddings browser Teratogenic disorder Neurodevelopmental Disorder

Fetal valproate syndrome is the pattern of congenital malformation, facial dysmorphism and neurodevelopmental impairment that follows exposure to valproic acid before birth. Maternal valproate therapy is the sole necessary cause, and the effect is dose-dependent: major malformations occur in about 10% of monotherapy-exposed pregnancies overall and about a quarter above 1500 mg daily. Three candidate upstream lesions are curated here in parallel rather than ranked — inhibition of class I histone deacetylases, antagonism of folate receptors and one-carbon metabolism, and a transient oxidising shift in embryonic redox state — because each has its own rescue or structure-activity evidence and none accounts for the whole phenotype. The 2019 European Reference Network consensus renamed the condition fetal valproate spectrum disorder to capture its range, and this entry treats the neurodevelopmental arm as the larger one: cognitive and social impairment is commoner than structural malformation and occurs without it.

Discussions and Knowledge Gaps

7
Three existing dismech entries record maternal valproate exposure and describe its mechanism differently. Anencephaly says the responsible step is unsettled between folate antagonism and histone deacetylase inhibition. Spina_Bifida_Cystica says valproate acts through HDAC inhibition independent of maternal folate status. Autism_Spectrum_Disorder links it to a molecular node with unknown intermediates. This entry curates three branches in parallel — HDAC inhibition, folate antagonism and redox disruption — without ranking them. Should the four entries be reconciled, and if so should the shared mechanism become a module rather than being restated in each?
CONTROVERSY OPEN fvs_mechanism_attribution_across_entries
Attached to
pathophysiology#Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
The disagreement is partly real and partly an artifact of each entry citing the source nearest to its own disease. Spina_Bifida_Cystica's independence claim rests on a review sentence calling HDAC inhibition a proposal, and it cites no folate study of its own — its sources there are PMID:27189655 and PMID:23790957. The case-control study usually offered for folate independence (PMID:20680999, cited in this entry but not in that one) explicitly declines to conclude that folate has no effect, and a blinded mouse rescue with folinic acid (PMID:3110521) points the other way. Meanwhile the strongest discriminating evidence is of a kind none of the three entries cites: structure-activity series across valproate derivatives in which HDAC inhibitory potency tracks teratogenic potency (PMID:16485903, PMID:15901671), and a parallel dissociation on the folate side where a non-teratogenic antiepileptic analogue leaves embryonic folate metabolism alone (PMID:1574172). Both branches have that form of evidence, which is why this entry ranks neither. A mechanism module would let the four entries share one account instead of maintaining four.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:16485903 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We also show a quantitative correlation between the IC50(HDAC) and the teratogenic potential of VPA derivatives, which clearly points toward HDACs as the formerly described teratogenic receptors of VPA-induced neural tube defects (NTDs)."
The structure-activity evidence on the HDAC side.
PMID:1574172 SUPPORT Model Organism
"A closely related structural analog of VPA (2-en-VPA, 2-propyl-2-pentenoic acid), which exhibits antiepileptic activity but not teratogenicity, did not influence the embryonic folate metabolism."
The equivalent dissociation on the folate side, which is why neither branch can be dismissed on this kind of evidence.
Does valproate push neural progenitors toward differentiation or hold them in the cycle? Human neural progenitors from 83 donors, human forebrain organoids and rat cortical progenitors all show reduced proliferation with premature differentiation. Mouse cortex in vivo shows the opposite: progenitors fail to exit the cycle on schedule, yielding excess superficial-layer neurons. Which describes human fetal cortex?
CONTROVERSY OPEN fvs_progenitor_direction_conflict
Attached to
pathophysiology#Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
This is not a minor discrepancy; the two accounts predict opposite cytoarchitecture. The species is one candidate explanation, the system another — dissociated or organoid culture against intact developing cortex — and exposure duration a third, since the human work is acute and the mouse work is in utero. The node is written as a disturbance of balance rather than a direction because of this, and the conflicting result is carried as a REFUTE item on the node rather than omitted. Resolving it would need a directional readout in intact human fetal tissue, which does not exist.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"This finding is consistent with VPA inducing a developmental shift in hNPCs, promoting early differentiation while suppressing proliferation."
One direction, in human cells.
PMID:27798144 SUPPORT Model Organism
"inhibits the cell cycle exit of neural progenitor cells during the early stage of neocortical histogenesis"
The other direction, in intact mouse cortex.
Rodent valproate models produce exencephaly, a cranial neural tube defect, while the characteristic human lesion is lumbosacral spina bifida. Does the difference in axial level limit what the rodent neural tube work establishes about the human defect?
HUMAN MODEL MISMATCH OPEN fvs_axial_level_mismatch
Attached to
pathophysiology#Failed Neural Tube Closure
Cranial and spinal neuropore closure use overlapping but distinct cellular mechanisms and close at different times, so a mechanism demonstrated for mouse exencephaly does not automatically transfer to human spina bifida. Most of the mechanistic evidence feeding this node — the folinic acid rescue, the NRF2 rescue, the folate metabolite work — is from models scoring exencephaly. The tension is already live inside dismech: Anencephaly.yaml records maternal valproate as TRIGGERS on failure of cranial neuropore closure in humans, so the repository asserts both a cranial and a lumbosacral human outcome for the same exposure, and the rodent evidence maps onto the cranial one. The human spinal cord organoid work (PMID:37643760) is the one system at the right axial level, and it is in vitro. The senescence study is informative here for a different reason: it found p19Arf mediated microcephaly but explicitly not exencephaly or spinal defects, which shows the two can come apart mechanistically within one model.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:34737154 SUPPORT Model Organism
"The prevalence of open neural tubes in embryos treated with VPA significantly decreased with D3T pretreatment, as did the severity of the observed defects evaluated by a morphological assessment."
The rescue is scored on open neural tubes in mouse, which in this model means cranial defects.
PMID:35700169 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Critically, through genetic and functional studies, we identified p19Arf as the instrumental mediator of senescence and microcephaly, but, surprisingly, not exencephaly and spinal defects."
Demonstrates within one model that the brain-growth and neural-tube arms are mechanistically separable, which is the general form of the concern here.
Is prenatal valproate associated with restricted fetal growth or microcephaly? A Nordic cohort of 4.5 million children found no association for either, and the consensus statement reports no significant increase in intrauterine growth restriction. The AAN practice guideline advises avoiding valproate partly to reduce small-for-gestational-age risk.
CONTROVERSY OPEN fvs_growth_disagreement
Attached to
phenotypes#Small for gestational age
The disagreement may be a vintage difference rather than a true conflict: the guideline's systematic review closed in August 2022 and the Nordic cohort published in 2024. It matters beyond the phenotype list, because the mouse senescence mechanism curated here produces microcephaly, and if microcephaly is not a human feature then that branch models something the human condition does not show. The phenotype is curated with evidence in both directions and the lowest frequency band rather than being asserted or dropped.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38476755 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"We identified 4,494,918 children (males: 51.3%, 2,306,991/4,494,918), including 38,714 (0.9%) children of mothers with epilepsy."
The size of the cohort finding no association, which is why it carries weight here.
Regulators have advised precaution about paternal valproate use before conception. Should paternal exposure be curated as an environmental factor in this entry?
OPEN QUESTION OPEN fvs_paternal_exposure
Attached to
environmental#Maternal valproate therapy during pregnancy
It is not curated here. The largest study available, pooling Norwegian and Taiwanese national cohorts, found the crude association disappeared once restricted to fathers with an indication for antiseizure medication and once compared against active comparators — the pattern of confounding by indication rather than of a causal effect. Several other 2025-2026 studies address the same question and were not assessed here. If a paternal effect were established it would not belong in this entry anyway, since fetal valproate syndrome is defined by fetal exposure; it would be a separate exposure-outcome relationship.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41733407 REFUTE Human Clinical
"In the indication-restricted PS-FSW analyses, the adjusted hazard ratios of NDDs among offspring exposed versus unexposed to valproate among fathers with an antiseizure medication indication were 1.20 (95% CI, 0.75 to 1.94) in Norway and 1.12 (95% CI, 0.92 to 1.35) in Taiwan."
The association does not survive restriction to fathers with the indication, which is the comparison that addresses confounding.
The blood methylation episignature of valproate-exposed individuals is enriched for cell-adhesion genes and overrepresents the cadherin superfamily. The pathophysiology here has a cell-junction node based on exposed human spinal cord organoids. Are these the same biology, and if so does the episignature record a persistent trace of the junctional lesion?
EMERGING HYPOTHESIS OPEN fvs_episignature_and_cell_adhesion
Attached to
pathophysiology#Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity
Neither source makes the connection; this entry noticed that they name the same gene families and is recording the observation as a hypothesis rather than curating an edge. The two are very far apart in tissue and time — blood in a living person against neuroepithelium during neurulation — so a shared gene-set enrichment is weak evidence of a shared mechanism and could reflect nothing more than cadherins being large, methylation-rich gene families. Testing it would mean asking whether the episignature is enriched for the specific junctional genes the organoids change.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:39097820 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Gene set enrichment analysis demonstrated an enrichment for terms associated with cell adhesion, including significant overrepresentation of the cadherin superfamily."
One half of the observation.
PMID:37643760 SUPPORT In Vitro
"we identified and verified the significant changes in cell-cell junctional genes/proteins in VPA-treated organoids using transcriptomic and immunostaining analysis"
The other half. The connection between them is this entry's, not either source's.
Sensory problems were reported in 80.6% of individuals with a diagnosis of fetal valproate spectrum disorder, which would make them among the commonest features, and the rat model independently shows altered sensory thresholds. No HPO term is bound for this in the entry. Which term should carry it?
CURATION TODO OPEN fvs_sensory_phenotype_unbound
Attached to
phenotypes#Autism
The source says "sensory problems" without specifying modality or direction, and HPO's candidates require a commitment the source does not make. Binding an approximate term to the second-commonest reported feature would be worse than leaving it visible as a gap. The rat model's finding — lower pain sensitivity and higher sensitivity to non-painful stimuli — is more specific but is a rodent result and cannot be used to bind a human phenotype. A source specifying the sensory phenotype in humans would close this.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37666366 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Rates of neurodevelopmental disorders, particularly autistic spectrum disorders (62.9%) and sensory problems (80.6%) are particularly central to the FVSD phenotype."
The unbound phenotype and its frequency, recorded here so the gap is visible rather than silent.

Pathophysiology

16
Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
Valproate in the maternal circulation crosses the placenta and reaches the embryo at concentrations above the maternal ones. This node exists so that the three candidate upstream lesions below have a shared parent: they are branches of one exposure, not three separate diseases, and the entry does not rank them.
embryo UBERON:0000922 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in embryo (UBERON:0000922). UBERON:0000922 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Class I Histone Deacetylase Inhibition Valproate inhibits HDAC1 at concentrations in the therapeutic range, so the enzyme is engaged directly by the drug that reaches the embryo.
Folate Receptor Antagonism Valproate binds the high-affinity folate receptors noncompetitively, which is a second drug-target interaction alongside the HDAC one. Calling the two branches parallel is a statement about how they are curated, not a claim that they are independent: valproate also lowers placental expression of folate carriers and one-carbon enzymes, which would make the folate lesion partly downstream of the transcriptional one. Both routes are recorded here and neither is ranked.
Embryonic Redox Disruption Exposed embryos shift transiently to a more oxidising glutathione redox potential. Marked indirect because no step is established between the drug and the redox change itself.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38203562 SUPPORT Other
"VPA crosses the human placenta and was found at higher concentrations in fetal blood."
Establishes that the embryo is exposed, and at what relative concentration. Source is OTHER because the citation is a narrative review.
PMID:38203562 SUPPORT Other
"Several teratogenic mechanisms are proposed for VPA, but the most important mechanisms seem to be its effects on the metabolism of folate, SAMe and histones, thus affecting DNA methylation."
A review naming the same branches this node feeds, and recording that they are proposals rather than a settled single mechanism.
Class I Histone Deacetylase Inhibition
Valproate inhibits class I histone deacetylases, with HDAC1 inhibited at about 0.4 mM. The case that this is a teratogenic mechanism and not merely a pharmacological property rests on structure-activity evidence rather than on correlation: across twenty valproate derivatives, HDAC inhibitory potency tracks teratogenic potency, non-teratogenic analogues do not inhibit the enzyme, and a structurally unrelated HDAC inhibitor reproduces the malformations.
HDAC1 hgnc:4852 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves HDAC1 (hgnc:4852). hgnc:4852 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
histone deacetylase activity GO:0004407 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this molecular function This pathophysiological event involves decreased histone deacetylase activity (GO:0004407). GO:0004407 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Embryonic Histone Hyperacetylation and Ectopic Transcription Removing deacetylase activity leaves histones hyperacetylated and derepresses transcription, which is the immediate consequence of the inhibition.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:16485903 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We also show a quantitative correlation between the IC50(HDAC) and the teratogenic potential of VPA derivatives, which clearly points toward HDACs as the formerly described teratogenic receptors of VPA-induced neural tube defects (NTDs)."
The structure-activity argument: potency against the enzyme predicts teratogenic potency across a derivative series.
PMID:16485903 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We demonstrate that only VPA derivatives with a teratogenic potential in mice are able to induce a hyperacetylation in core histone H4 in teratocarcinoma F9 cells."
The converse arm: non-teratogenic analogues fail to produce the mark.
PMID:15901671 SUPPORT Model Organism
"We found that VPA and other HDAC inhibitors cause very similar and characteristic developmental defects whereas VPA analogs with poor inhibitory activity in vivo have little teratogenic effect."
The same dissociation in whole embryos, in two species, with an unrelated HDAC inhibitor as the positive comparator.
Embryonic Histone Hyperacetylation and Ectopic Transcription
In the exposed embryo the acetylation mark rises within hours and is not uniform: it is concentrated in the neuroepithelium, heart and somites, which are the tissues that go on to malform. Other histone marks move with it while global DNA methylation does not, so the proximate lesion is chromatin state rather than methylation. In human neural progenitors the drug reaches two-thirds of all measured chromatin peaks, and — against the expectation for an HDAC inhibitor — closes more regions than it opens.
neuroepithelial stem cell CL:0002259 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neuroepithelial stem cell (CL:0002259). CL:0002259 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
epigenetic regulation of gene expression GO:0040029 ↕ DYSREGULATED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves dysregulated epigenetic regulation of gene expression (GO:0040029). GO:0040029 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. chromatin remodeling GO:0006338 ↕ DYSREGULATED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves dysregulated chromatin remodeling (GO:0006338). GO:0006338 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
developing neuroepithelium UBERON:0034705 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in developing neuroepithelium (UBERON:0034705). UBERON:0034705 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Experimental models
Genotyped human neural progenitor population exposed to valproic acid PRIMARY CELL CULTURE Measures the chromatin response directly in human cells, and finds it is not the simple opening that HDAC inhibition alone would predict.
Downstream
Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation In human neural progenitors the same exposure that remodels chromatin shifts the cells out of the cycle toward differentiation, with the closed regions enriched for proliferative transcription-factor motifs and the opened ones for neuronal differentiation. The cited experiment measures both changes under one exposure rather than showing that the first causes the second, and transcription necessarily intervenes, so the link is recorded as indirect with unknown intermediates.
Disturbed Cranial Neural Crest Formation and Migration Exposure misplaces Hoxa2 expression and lowers Sox9 in the premigratory cranial crest. The transcriptional misregulation is consistent with the chromatin lesion, but no cited experiment connects the acetylation mark to these specific genes, so the intermediates are unknown.
Neuroepithelial Senescence Exposed neuroepithelium induces senescence through p19Arf. The route from chromatin state to p19Arf induction is not established in the cited work.
Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity Cell-cell junction genes and proteins change in exposed human spinal cord organoids. The link to the chromatin lesion is inferred from the exposure being the same, not demonstrated.
Impaired Dendritic Morphogenesis and Synapse Maturation In human neurons the drug's HDAC and GSK-3 inhibition downregulates dendritic genes including MARCKSL1, which is named as the mediator — so an intermediate is identified rather than merely postulated.
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:20705080 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Immunohistochemical staining revealed increased histone acetylation in the neuroepithelium, heart, and somites."
The tissue distribution of the mark, which matches the organs that malform and is why this node is not merely a cell-culture observation.
PMID:20705080 SUPPORT Model Organism
"No significant differences in global or CpG island DNA methylation were observed in embryo homogenates."
Bounds the claim: the measurable embryonic change is in histone marks, not in global DNA methylation.
PMID:16193500 SUPPORT Model Organism
"A direct correlation between somite hyperacetylation and axial abnormalities could suggest the HDAC inhibition as the mechanism of the teratogenic effects."
Links the mark in a specific tissue to the malformation of that tissue, stated as the authors state it.
+ 2 more references
Folate Receptor Antagonism
Valproate binds the high-affinity folate receptors noncompetitively, so exposed cells take up less folate. This is the drug-target step of the folate branch, separated from the embryonic metabolic consequence below because the two are measured in different systems and at different scales: receptor binding in cells, metabolite pools in whole embryos.
FOLR1 hgnc:3791 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves FOLR1 (hgnc:3791). hgnc:3791 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.

GO:1904447 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
folic acid receptor activity GO:0061714 ↓ DECREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this molecular function This pathophysiological event involves decreased folic acid receptor activity (GO:0061714). GO:0061714 is a molecular function from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism Reduced folate uptake is taken to produce the embryonic metabolite shift. The receptor work is in a kidney-derived cell line and the metabolite work in mouse embryos, so no single experiment connects them; the authors of the receptor study state the link conditionally themselves.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:25066307 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Tests in HEK293T cells indicate that the membrane-bound folate receptors of VPA treated cells bind significantly lower amounts of folic acid than do untreated cells."
The functional consequence of the binding, measured in cells.
Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism
In the exposed embryo the pattern of folate metabolites shifts, with formyl-tetrahydrofolates falling and tetrahydrofolate rising, attributed to inhibited transfer of the formyl group. The structure-activity argument applies on this branch as it does on the HDAC one: an analogue with antiepileptic activity but no teratogenic activity leaves embryonic folate metabolism alone. MTHFR is named here because the maternal 677C>T genotype modifies this branch's susceptibility, and the molecular function it reduces is what connects the gene to the metabolic disturbance: the knock-in mouse carrying that variant shows lower MTHFR enzymatic activity and raised homocysteine. The association and its limits are curated in the genetic section.
MTHFR hgnc:7436 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this gene This pathophysiological event involves MTHFR (hgnc:7436). hgnc:7436 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.

Animal models
Mthfr 677C>T knock-in mouse with valproate exposure Mus musculus perturbs Tests the folate branch by varying one-carbon capacity genetically instead of varying the drug, which is the more informative direction.
Downstream
Failed Neural Tube Closure Lower folate availability during neurulation is proposed to cause closure failure. The steps between the metabolite shift and the tissue failure are not established, and the human supplementation evidence does not confirm the route, so this edge is deliberately weak.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:1574172 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Teratogenic doses of valproic acid altered the pattern of folate metabolites in the embryo: Levels of 5-formyl- and 10-formyl-tetrahydrofolates decreased, and the level of tetrahydrofolate increased."
The metabolic disturbance in the embryo itself, at teratogenic doses, during the neurulation window.
PMID:1574172 SUPPORT Model Organism
"These changes could be explained by VPA-mediated inhibition of transfer of the formyl group via glutamate formyltransferase."
The enzymatic step the authors propose for the metabolite shift.
PMID:1574172 SUPPORT Model Organism
"A closely related structural analog of VPA (2-en-VPA, 2-propyl-2-pentenoic acid), which exhibits antiepileptic activity but not teratogenicity, did not influence the embryonic folate metabolism."
The dissociation that separates the teratogenic property from the antiepileptic one on this branch.
Embryonic Redox Disruption
Exposed embryos show a transiently oxidising glutathione redox potential and raised reactive oxygen species concentrated in the head region, with apoptosis tracking the oxidant signal. The branch is curated because it carries pharmacological rescue in two independent directions — an NRF2 inducer given beforehand, and catalase in culture — rather than because the association is strong.
response to oxidative stress GO:0006979 ↑ INCREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased response to oxidative stress (GO:0006979). GO:0006979 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology. apoptotic process GO:0006915 ↑ INCREASED Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves increased apoptotic process (GO:0006915). GO:0006915 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Animal models
Mouse neural tube defect model with NRF2 induction Mus musculus rescues Intervening on the antioxidant response, not on the drug, reduces both the redox shift and the defect.
Downstream
Failed Neural Tube Closure Pretreatment with an NRF2 inducer prevents both the redox shift and a substantial part of the closure failure, which is the strongest argument that the redox change is on the causal path rather than beside it.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:21868484 SUPPORT Model Organism
"VPA exposure resulted in an increase in ROS staining in the head region, as assessed by whole-mount staining with 5-(and-6)-chloromethyl-2',7'-dichlorodihydrofluorescein diacetate."
Localizes the oxidant signal to the region where the defects arise.
PMID:21868484 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Addition of catalase to the medium attenuated VPA-induced increases in ROS formation and apoptosis."
An antioxidant rescue of both the oxidant signal and the cell death, which is what makes this a candidate mechanism rather than a correlate.
PMID:21868484 REFUTE Model Organism
"Markers of embryonic oxidative damage including 8-hydroxyguanosine, 4-hydroxynonenal adducts, and 3-nitrotyrosine were not affected by VPA treatment."
The same study found no measurable oxidative damage to macromolecules, which argues against a damage-mediated version of this branch and is carried so the node is not read as established oxidative injury.
Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity
Exposure changes the cell-cell junction programme of neurulating human neuroepithelium, and the mouse embryo shows the same gene changes alongside the defect. A related human organoid result puts the failure specifically at apical constriction, where valproate and SHROOM3 loss converge. This is the cellular branch closest to the mechanics of closure.
neuroepithelial stem cell CL:0002259 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this cell type This pathophysiological event involves neuroepithelial stem cell (CL:0002259). CL:0002259 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.

neural tube UBERON:0001049 Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology (UBERON) Relation: this pathophysiological event occurs in this anatomical location This pathophysiological event occurs in neural tube (UBERON:0001049). UBERON:0001049 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Experimental models
Human spinal cord organoid model of valproate-induced neurulation failure ORGANOID Identifies the junctional programme as the thing that changes, in human tissue, during the process that fails.
Human single-rosette brain organoid model of apical constriction failure ORGANOID Measures apical constriction directly, by lumen size, and shows a teratogenic and a genetic cause of neural tube defects converging on it.
Downstream
Failed Neural Tube Closure Apical constriction is the cell behaviour that bends the neural plate, so its impairment is a mechanical account of closure failure, with the intervening step — neural plate bending — identified rather than unknown. Not marked direct because the cited evidence is a hedged inference from lumen size in a brain organoid in which no neural tube either closed or failed to close.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37643760 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Furthermore, VPA-treated mouse embryos exhibited impaired gene expression and NTD phenotypes, similar to those observed in the hSCO model."
Cross-validates the human organoid finding in an intact embryo, which is what lifts it above an in-vitro observation.
PMID:37443734 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The increased lumen sizes were caused by reduced cell apical constriction suggesting that impingement of this process is a shared mechanism for VPA treatment and SHROOM3-KO, two well-known causes of NTDs."
Identifies the specific cell-shape process and shows a teratogen and a genetic neural tube defect cause converging on it.
Failed Neural Tube Closure
The convergence point of the folate, redox and junctional branches: the neural tube does not close. In humans the resulting lesion is characteristically lumbosacral spina bifida, with a twelve-fold odds ratio after first-trimester monotherapy — the largest single malformation signal in the syndrome and the one that first identified valproate as a teratogen.
neural tube closure GO:0001843 ⚠ ABNORMAL Gene Ontology (GO) Relation: this pathophysiological event involves this biological process This pathophysiological event involves abnormal neural tube closure (GO:0001843). GO:0001843 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.

UBERON:0001049 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Downstream
Spina bifida The human lesion closure failure produces, characteristically lumbosacral.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:20558369 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The use of valproic acid in the first trimester of pregnancy is associated with an increased risk of spina bifida, but data on the risks of other congenital malformations are limited."
The human outcome this node produces, and the timing window that places it at neurulation.
PMID:38497990 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Valproate exposure was associated with a wide range of MCMs, which also included hypospadias, neural tube defects, and multiple defects."
Confirms the neural tube outcome in the current prospective registry.
Disturbed Cranial Neural Crest Formation and Migration
Exposure just before crest formation reduces delamination of the midbrain-rhombomere 1/2 crest and then misroutes rhombomere 4 crest into territories that should be Hox-free. These are the cells that build the frontonasal mass and first branchial arch, which is the anatomical basis for the facial gestalt and for the palate.

Animal models
Rat cranial neural crest exposure model Rattus norvegicus recapitulates Shows both halves of the node — reduced crest formation at midbrain-rhombomere 1/2 and misrouted rhombomere 4 crest carrying the wrong positional identity.
Downstream
Craniofacial Dysmorphogenesis The affected crest populations are the ones that migrate to the frontonasal mass and first branchial arch, which is where the face is built. The step to the facial gestalt is this entry's inference and not the authors': the outcomes they name for this mechanism are trigeminal axonal elongation and reduced head size, and reduced head size is something this entry records as not established in humans. The edge is therefore marked indirect with unknown intermediates rather than direct.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38403785 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Hoxa2-positive NCCs were detected normally in BA2 and abnormally in FNM and BA1, which are normally Hox-free, implying VPA-induced abnormal cranial NCC migration."
The misrouting itself, identified by cells carrying the wrong positional marker in the wrong arch.
Craniofacial Dysmorphogenesis
The structural result in the face and skull: the recognizable gestalt, cleft palate — predominantly isolated rather than with cleft lip — and metopic suture ridging that at its extreme is craniosynostosis. Recorded as a tissue outcome rather than as a list of phenotypes so the crest mechanism has somewhere to land.

The consensus description of the gestalt this node produces.
PMID:26408639 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"ICP is the predominant cleft type seen in prenatal VPA exposure."
The clefting is specifically of the palate rather than lip and palate, which the authors note bears on the teratogenic mechanism.
Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
Exposure changes the balance between progenitor self-renewal and differentiation, and the direction of the change is not consistent across systems. Human neural progenitors from 83 donors and human forebrain organoids both lose proliferative capacity; rat cortical progenitors differentiate prematurely through a beta-catenin-Ras-ERK-p21 route; but mouse cortex in vivo shows the opposite, with progenitors failing to exit the cycle on schedule. The node is curated as a disturbance of the balance rather than as a direction, and the disagreement is carried in the attached evidence and in a discussion.

Experimental models
Genotyped human neural progenitor population exposed to valproic acid PRIMARY CELL CULTURE The proliferation reduction is present in every donor at 1 mM, so it is a property of the exposure rather than of a susceptible subset.
Human dorsal forebrain organoid model of outer radial glia depletion ORGANOID Shows the progenitor deficit in a human three-dimensional system and localizes it to the subtype that expands the human cortex.
Downstream
Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis Whichever direction the balance shifts, the cortex is built from the wrong number of cells at the wrong time; the mouse work states the outcome as neocortical dysgenesis.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"VPA exposure leads to consistent reductions in proliferation across all donors at clinically relevant concentrations"
The effect is present in every donor at a clinically relevant concentration, which is what makes this a property of the exposure rather than of a genotype.
PMID:36523605 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We found that VPA exposure in organoids caused a reduction in the size and impairment in the proliferation and expansion of neural progenitor cells (NPCs) in a dose-dependent manner."
The same direction in a three-dimensional human model, and dose-dependently.
PMID:19068119 SUPPORT In Vitro
"We report here that 1 mM VPA simultaneously induces differentiation and reduces proliferation of basic fibroblast growth factor (bFGF)-treated embryonic day 14 (E14) rat cerebral cortex neural progenitor cells (NPCs)."
Names the signalling route for the differentiation arm, at the same concentration as the human progenitor work.
+ 1 more reference
Neuroepithelial Senescence
Exposed neuroepithelium induces cellular senescence, and genetic dissection identifies p19Arf as the mediator. The same work makes an unusually clean negative claim: p19Arf accounts for the brain-growth deficit but not for exencephaly or the spinal defects, so this node feeds the neurogenesis arm only and is deliberately not connected to neural tube closure.

GO:0022008 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis Senescent neuroepithelial cells are permanently arrested, and the paper locates them in tissue where neurogenesis is already defective.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:35700169 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Critically, through genetic and functional studies, we identified p19Arf as the instrumental mediator of senescence and microcephaly, but, surprisingly, not exencephaly and spinal defects."
Establishes the mediator and, in the same sentence, bounds which outcomes it explains — which is why this node has one downstream target and not two.
PMID:35700169 SUPPORT Model Organism
"we identified p19Arf as the instrumental mediator of senescence and microcephaly"
Names the mediator, quoted as a mouse-genetics result rather than from the paper's mixed mouse-and-organoid summary sentence, so the evidence_source is unambiguous.
Impaired Dendritic Morphogenesis and Synapse Maturation
In human neurons, exposure impairs dendritic morphology and function through HDAC and GSK-3 inhibition with MARCKSL1 as a named mediator. The finding that matters clinically is the restriction: immature neurons are damaged and mature ones are not. That makes this the branch that explains why exposure after organogenesis still harms neurodevelopment, and why the neurodevelopmental phenotype extends beyond children with malformations.

Experimental models
Directly reprogrammed human neurons with staged valproate exposure IPSC DERIVED MODEL Establishes the developmental restriction by direct comparison of immature and mature neurons in the same system.
Downstream
Altered Neural Circuit Formation Dendrites and synapses are the substrate of circuits, and the protein named here is described as essential for exactly those processes.

PMID:31155484 SUPPORT In Vitro
"VPA exposure caused chronic impairment of dendritic morphology and functional properties of developing neurons, but not those of mature neurons."
The developmental restriction, which is the substance of this node rather than the impairment alone.
PMID:31155484 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Our findings identify a developmentally restricted pathogenic mechanism of VPA and establish the use of reprogrammed neurons as an effective platform for modeling teratogenic pathways."
The authors' summary of the mechanism as developmentally restricted.
Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis
The cortex is assembled with altered progenitor output: fewer outer radial glia in human organoids, excess superficial-layer projection neurons in mouse, and reduced Purkinje cell number in the rat cerebellum. The lamination and cell-number abnormalities are the tissue-level bridge between the progenitor disturbance and the behavioural outcome.

UBERON:0000956 is an anatomical location from the Uberon multi-species anatomy ontology.
Animal models
Prenatal valproate rat model of autism Rattus norvegicus recapitulates The same exposure and timing reduce Purkinje cell number in the cerebellar vermis, with the posterior lobe affected more than the anterior — a regional gradient rather than a global loss.
Downstream
Altered Neural Circuit Formation Building the cortex and cerebellum with the wrong cell numbers and laminar distribution is taken to alter the circuits they form. This edge carries no evidence of its own on purpose: no cited experiment follows a specific cytoarchitectural change through to a circuit measurement, and the one sentence that might have been used here — that the rat cerebellar findings parallel human autism neuropathology — is a cross-species validity remark about Purkinje counts, not a statement about this step. It is attached to the source node instead.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:27798144 SUPPORT Model Organism
"increases the production of projection neurons distributed in the superficial neocortical layers in embryonic brains"
A specific laminar abnormality, not simply "abnormal cortex".
PMID:10840175 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Compared to controls, rats exposed to a single dose of 600-mg/kg sodium valproate on embryonic day 12.5 had significantly fewer Purkinje cells in the cerebellar vermis and a reduction short of significant in the hemispheres."
Extends the neurogenesis deficit beyond the neocortex, with a regional gradient within the cerebellum.
PMID:10840175 SUPPORT Model Organism
"The results parallel those reported for human cases of autism."
A cross-species validity remark about the Purkinje finding. Attached to this node rather than to any causal edge, because it compares neuropathology and does not assert a step.
Altered Neural Circuit Formation
Circuits assembled from disordered neurogenesis and from neurons whose dendrites and synapses matured abnormally. This node is the circuit-level step only; the clinical consequence is the separate organism-scale node below, because no cited experiment follows a circuit measurement through to a cognitive score and bundling the two would hide that gap.

GO:0048813 is a biological process from the Gene Ontology.
Downstream
Neurodevelopmental Impairment The step from altered circuits to measured cognition and behaviour. It carries no evidence because none exists in the cited literature: the human evidence for the outcome is epidemiological, and the mechanistic evidence stops at the cellular and tissue level. The edge is asserted as the entry's inference and labelled as such.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Gene regulatory responses to valproic acid (VPA), which is also a prenatal risk factor for autism, and lithium were highly enriched in genetic risk for psychiatric disorders, demonstrating the convergence of environmental and genetic factors."
Connects the molecular response in human progenitors to the same genetic risk that underlies the clinical outcomes, which is the closest cited link between the mechanism and the phenotype.
Neurodevelopmental Impairment
The clinical outcome of the neurodevelopmental arm: reduced IQ with a disproportionate verbal deficit, language impairment, autism and ADHD. The evidence here is epidemiological and dose-dependent rather than mechanistic, which is why it sits in its own node rather than inside the circuit-level one.
Animal models
Prenatal valproate rat model of autism Rattus norvegicus partially recapitulates Reproduces the behavioural end of the neurodevelopmental arm, including the sensory abnormalities that the human cohort data put at 80% of diagnosed individuals. Pointed at the organism-scale outcome node rather than at circuit formation, because what this model observes is behaviour.
Downstream
Cognitive impairment The commonest outcome, and the one that occurs without any structural malformation.
Intellectual disability The severe end of the cognitive distribution.
Delayed speech and language development The earliest-presenting feature, and the one MONDO singles out.
Language impairment The persisting form of the verbal deficit.
Autism The neurodevelopmental diagnosis most strongly associated with the exposure.
Attention deficit hyperactivity disorder Raised specifically by valproate among antiseizure medications.
Motor delay Gross and fine motor delay accompany the cognitive picture.
Specific learning disability The educational consequence of the cognitive profile.
Memory impairment One of the domains measured below expectation in both cohorts.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23352199 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"age-6 IQ was lower after exposure to valproate (mean 97, 95% CI 94-101) than to carbamazepine"
The human cognitive outcome, measured against other antiseizure medications rather than against no exposure, which controls for maternal epilepsy.
PMID:23352199 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"High doses of valproate were negatively associated with IQ (r=-0·56, p<0·0001)"
The dose-response for the cognitive outcome, which is the feature of the human evidence that most supports causation.

Genetic Associations

MTHFR (Maternal 677C>T genotype as a susceptibility modifier; the child's genotype shows no effect)

Medical Actions

6
Avoidance of valproate before conception
Category: Therapeutic Action: Preventive InterventionNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Preventive Intervention (NCIT:C15843). NCIT:C15843 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15843
Platform: Other
The only intervention that prevents the condition, and the reason the entry records a HIGH burden for something entirely avoidable. Guidance is directive: avoid valproate in people of childbearing potential where clinically feasible, and use lamotrigine, levetiracetam or oxcarbazepine instead. Timing is the whole of it. In Australian register data, malformation rates were 4.5% where valproate was reduced or withdrawn before pregnancy against 10.9% where it was unchanged, and 2.7% in the subgroup where it was stopped altogether. The cost falls on seizure control: in that same changed group — the 4.5% one, not the 2.7% subgroup — seizure-affected pregnancies rose from 31% to 46%. This is a trade, not a free improvement. Once a pregnancy has started the calculus reverses, and the guideline cautions against removing an effective drug.
Mechanism Target:
Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
Show evidence (5 references)
PMID:38748979 SUPPORT Other
"Clinicians must avoid the use of valproic acid in PWECP to minimize the risk of MCMs or neural tube defects (NTDs), if clinically feasible."
The guideline's strongest-level recommendation on the malformation arm.
PMID:38748979 SUPPORT Other
"Clinicians must consider using lamotrigine, levetiracetam, or oxcarbazepine in PWECP when appropriate based on the patient's epilepsy syndrome, likelihood of achieving seizure control, and comorbidities, to minimize the risk of MCMs."
Names the alternatives, which is what makes avoidance actionable.
PMID:32314363 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Although the available data have limitations, fetal malformation rates in the pregnancies studied were lower in the VPA changed pregnancies (4.5%) than in the VPA unchanged comparator pregnancies (10.9%, hazard ratio [HR] 0.412, 95% confidence interval [CI] 0.190-0.892), and were only 2.7% where..."
Quantifies the benefit of stopping before conception.

Periconceptional folic acid supplementation
Category: Therapeutic Action: Preventative Nutritional SupplementationNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Preventative Nutritional Supplementation (NCIT:C64894). NCIT:C64894 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C64894
Agent: folic acid CHEBI:27470 Chemical Entities of Biological Interest (CHEBI) Relation: this treatment uses this therapeutic agent This treatment uses folic acid (CHEBI:27470). CHEBI:27470 is a therapeutic agent from Chemical Entities of Biological Interest.
Platform: Small molecule
Recommended universally for people on antiseizure medication, with the consensus specifying 0.4-5 mg daily from two to three months before conception until twelve weeks. The recommendation is firmer than the evidence behind it for this particular exposure: high-dose folic acid before pregnancy reduced malformations across antiseizure medications generally, while the consensus calls the evidence for preventing valproate teratogenesis conflicting and a case-control study found no protection against valproate-associated spina bifida.
Mechanism Target:
Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:38748979 SUPPORT Other
"Clinicians should prescribe at least 0.4 mg of folic acid supplementation daily preconceptionally and during pregnancy to any PWECP treated with an ASM"
The guideline recommendation and its minimum dose.
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"It is recommended that folic acid, taken in doses 0.4-5 mg daily should be taken from 2 to 3 months before conception and should be continued until 12 weeks of pregnancy."
The dose range and the window.
PMID:31324220 REFUTE Other
"The evidence of high dose folic acid in preventing VPA teratogenesis is conflicting"
The same consensus statement's verdict on the evidence, carried so the recommendation is not read as settled efficacy.
Surgical correction of structural malformations
Category: Therapeutic Action: Surgical ProcedureNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Surgical Procedure (NCIT:C15329). NCIT:C15329 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15329
Platform: Surgery
Repair of the malformations as they arise: neural tube defect closure, cleft palate repair, hypospadias repair, cardiac repair, and craniofacial surgery for metopic synostosis. The craniosynostosis case is the one where timing has been argued to matter beyond the structural result — in the valproate-exposed trigonocephaly series, IQ was higher in those operated before six months, which is an observational association in a small series rather than a demonstrated cognitive benefit.

Multidisciplinary developmental and medical surveillance
Category: Monitoring Action: Supportive CareNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Supportive Care (NCIT:C15747). NCIT:C15747 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15747
Platform: Other
Not a treatment so much as the organizing principle of care: scheduled review of growth, development, hearing and vision, neuropsychological assessment across cognition, language, memory, attention and executive function, and autism screening at around eighteen months. The consensus exists because this coordination was absent, and it says plainly that the management literature is lacking — so this is expert opinion, not trial evidence.

Genetic counselling and recurrence risk discussion
Category: Counseling / Informational Action: Genetic CounselingNCI Thesaurus (NCIT) Relation: this treatment is this clinical intervention This treatment is Genetic Counseling (NCIT:C15240). NCIT:C15240 is a clinical intervention from the NCI Thesaurus. NCIT:C15240
Platform: Other
Two distinct conversations. For the affected individual there is no heritable risk to pass on. For the mother, a previous affected child raises the risk in a subsequent pregnancy — 16.8% after one malformed child and 50% after two in the UK register, with a non-significant trend to higher recurrence specifically for valproate.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:23167802 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"For women whose first child had a congenital malformation there was a 16.8% risk of having another child with a congenital malformation, compared with 9.8% for women whose first child did not have a malformation (relative risk 1.73, 95% confidence interval [CI] 1.01-2.96)."
The recurrence figure that the counselling conveys.
PMID:23167802 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"There was a trend toward a higher risk for recurrent malformations in pregnancies exposed to valproate (21.9%, relative risk 1.47, 95% CI 0.68-3.20)"
The valproate-specific recurrence, with an interval crossing one, which is why the general figure is the one counselled.

Environmental Factors

3
Maternal valproate therapy during pregnancy
gestational maternal valproate exposure XCO:0001598 Experimental Conditions Ontology (XCO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is gestational maternal valproate exposure, annotated with gestational maternal exposure to valproate (XCO:0001598). XCO:0001598 is an exposure from the Experimental Conditions Ontology.
The exposure term is XCO:0001598, the same binding the Autism_Spectrum_Disorder and Anencephaly entries use, so those three records can be queried together. Spina_Bifida_Cystica will not be found by that query: it binds ECTO:9001740 (exposure to anticonvulsant), a drug-class term rather than a valproate one, even though it is the entry most directly about the valproate neural tube signal. ECTO has no gestational maternal valproate exposure term: it carries ECTO:9002607 (exposure to sodium valproate) and ECTO:0900506 (maternal exposure to valproate semisodium), neither of which is maternal gestational exposure to valproate itself.
Valproic acid and its salts, taken for epilepsy, bipolar disorder or migraine prophylaxis, reaching the embryo by placental transfer. This is the sole necessary cause: the syndrome does not occur without it. Two features matter for the rest of the entry. It is dose-dependent, with no identified risk-free dose. And it is not explained by the maternal illness, because malformations follow valproate taken for indications other than epilepsy — which is what licenses treating the drug rather than the epilepsy as the cause.
Show evidence (4 references)
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"A pattern of major and minor congenital anomalies, facial dysmorphic features, and neurodevelopmental difficulties, including cognitive and social impairments has been reported in some children exposed to sodium valproate (VPA) during pregnancy."
The consensus statement's definition of the condition as the consequence of this exposure.
PMID:38203562 SUPPORT Other
"It induces malformations even when taken for indications other than epilepsy, adding to the data that epilepsy is not responsible for the teratogenic effects."
Separates the drug from the maternal illness, which is the confounder that would otherwise explain the association.
PMID:38497990 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"For valproate, phenobarbital, and carbamazepine, there was a significant increase in the prevalence of MCMs associated with increasing dose of the ASM."
Establishes dose dependence in the largest prospective registry.
+ 1 more reference
Mechanism Target:
TRIGGERS Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo — Maternal dosing delivers the drug to the embryo directly, at fetal concentrations exceeding maternal ones, which is the initiating step of every branch curated here.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:38203562 SUPPORT Other
"VPA crosses the human placenta and was found at higher concentrations in fetal blood."
States the transfer and the concentration gradient that make the embryo the exposed compartment. Source is OTHER because the citation is a narrative review reporting the finding, not a primary pharmacokinetic study.
Periconceptional high-dose folic acid supplementation
exposure to folic acid ECTO:9000123 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is exposure to folic acid (ECTO:9000123). ECTO:9000123 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
The folic acid evidence is not valproate-specific where it is strongest (PMID:41876129 pools antiseizure medications) and not protective where it is valproate-specific (PMID:20680999, spina bifida). A third result bears on the neurodevelopmental arm rather than on malformations: among antiepileptic-exposed children, autistic traits were commoner when the mother had not supplemented (PMID:29279889).
Folic acid taken before conception and through early pregnancy. Recorded as protective with its own contradiction attached, because the evidence separates by outcome and by timing. Across antiseizure medications, high-dose folic acid started in the twelve weeks before pregnancy reduced malformations while the same supplement started after pregnancy onset did not. For spina bifida specifically in valproate-exposed pregnancies, the protective effect seen in unexposed pregnancies was not detected, on an interval too wide to exclude one. The consensus statement calls the evidence for preventing valproate teratogenesis conflicting, and this entry records it that way rather than presenting folate as a solution.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41876129 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Initiation of high-dose folic acid in the 12 weeks before pregnancy was associated with a reduced risk of MCA in offspring of women using ASMs while initiation in the 12 weeks after pregnancy onset was not."
The timing contrast, in a target-trial emulation across antiseizure medications rather than valproate alone.
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"The evidence of high dose folic acid in preventing VPA teratogenesis is conflicting"
The consensus group's own verdict, which is why this exposure carries evidence in both directions.
Mechanism Target:
PROTECTS_AGAINST Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism — Supplementation is directed at the folate branch, and the animal rescue experiments act on it, but the human evidence does not establish that the observed protection runs through this node rather than through folate-dependent processes generally. Attached to this node rather than to the shared downstream outcome because it is the branch the intervention targets.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:3110521 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Folinic acid (5-CHO-THF) coadministration by i.p. injection or by a constant rate infusion via osmotic minipumps, implanted s.c., significantly reduced the exencephaly rates using a randomized double-blind experimental procedure."
A blinded rescue in the mouse, the strongest evidence that supplying reduced folate counteracts the exposure.
PMID:20680999 REFUTE Human Clinical
"The ORs for the effect of correct folic acid use were calculated among antiepileptic drug (AED) unexposed pregnancies 0.5 [95%CI: 0.3-0.7] and among VPA exposed pregnancies 1.0 [95%CI: 0.1-7.6]."
The protective effect present in unexposed pregnancies is absent in valproate-exposed ones for spina bifida. Carried as a refutation rather than omitted, though the interval is wide enough that the study itself declines to call it a null result.
Antiseizure medication polytherapy
anticonvulsant exposure during pregnancy ECTO:9001740 Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology (ECTO) Relation: this environmental factor is this exposure This environmental factor is anticonvulsant exposure during pregnancy, annotated with exposure to anticonvulsant (ECTO:9001740). ECTO:9001740 is an exposure from the Environmental Conditions, Treatments and Exposures Ontology.
Valproate combined with another antiseizure medication. Recorded as modulating rather than exacerbating, because the EURAP analysis found the malformation rate tracked valproate dose rather than the combination: polytherapy marks higher risk without being shown to add risk of its own, and nothing cited claims co-medication increases placental valproate transfer.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26085607 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The frequency of MCMs at 1 year after birth was 10.0% for VPA monotherapy, 11.3% for exposures to VPA and LTG, and 11.7% for exposures to VPA + another (non-LTG) AED."
The rates by combination, higher than monotherapy but close enough that the study attributes them to dose.
Mechanism Target:
MODULATES Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo — Co-medication raises the observed malformation rate, but the same analysis attributes the increase primarily to valproate dose, so no mechanism is asserted for the combination itself.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:26085607 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The risk of MCMs associated with VPA exposure increases with increasing VPA dose, both in the presence and in the absence of one concomitant AED, and appears to be related primarily to the dose of VPA."
Supports the exposure being graded by valproate dose and simultaneously limits what polytherapy explains.
🔬

Diagnosis

4
Documented valproate exposure during pregnancy with exclusion of alternatives
The diagnosis is clinical and has no confirmatory test. It requires documented exposure, a compatible pattern, and the exclusion of other explanations — in practice a normal chromosomal microarray and Fragile X study. The exclusion step is not a formality: in one audit of 40 exposed individuals, six had a different genetic cause of their developmental delay, and the majority did not meet the threshold for the diagnosis at all.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"The diagnosis of FVSD is difficult, as there is no specific biomarker that can be assayed in this condition to prove diagnosis, though there are conditions with overlapping features which need to be excluded"
States both the absence of a test and the need for exclusion.
PMID:35863137 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Six of the forty patients (15%) had an alternative genetic cause of developmental delay established."
Quantifies how often exposure plus delay turns out to have another cause, which is the reason exclusion is required rather than advisable.
PMID:35863137 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"An equal awareness is warranted that developmental delay in the context of VPA exposure in pregnancy does not necessarily constitute a diagnosis of FVSD but that other competing diagnostic hypotheses have to be considered."
The authors' statement of the inferential error this criterion guards against.
Facial gestalt recognition
The facial pattern makes the diagnosis more secure but is explicitly not required for it, particularly after a low dose or after exposure beginning later than facial development. This is the clinical counterpart of the entry's ordering: the neurodevelopmental arm is the larger one and does not depend on the face.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"Due to these points the expert consensus group felt that whilst the presence of a typical facial presentation makes the diagnosis more certain, typical facial features are not absolutely required to be able to diagnose that an individual has been affected by exposure to VPA in utero,..."
The consensus position on how much diagnostic weight the face carries.
Peripheral blood DNA methylation episignature
A classifier trained on blood methylation distinguishes exposed affected individuals from controls and from a reference set of other syndromes with episignatures. It is the first objective candidate biomarker for a condition that has none, and it is a research tool rather than standard care. Its enrichment for cadherin-superfamily cell-adhesion genes is suggestive beside the cell-junction mechanism curated in the pathophysiology, but neither source connects them.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:39097820 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Genomic DNA methylation analysis was applied, and a distinct DNA methylation profile was identified in the majority of affected individuals."
The signature and its coverage — the majority, not all, which bounds its use as a test.
PMID:39097820 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The valproate exposure episignature exhibited high sensitivity and specificity relative to a large reference data set of unaffected controls and individuals with a wide spectrum of syndromic disorders with episignatures."
The discrimination that matters clinically is against other syndromes, not only against healthy controls.
PMID:39097820 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Gene set enrichment analysis demonstrated an enrichment for terms associated with cell adhesion, including significant overrepresentation of the cadherin superfamily."
The enriched gene set, recorded because it names the same biology as the cell-junction node without the paper making that link.
Targeted fetal anomaly ultrasound and structured postnatal examination
Prenatally, detailed scanning with the sonographer told about the exposure. Postnatally, a directed examination for the specific anomalies rather than a general newborn check — direct visualization of the palate including submucous cleft, radial ray, talipes and metopic ridging.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"Special care should be taken to visualise the palate directly and to check for submucous cleft palate, to look for radial ray defects, talipes and ridging of the metopic suture indicating metopic synostosis."
The specific postnatal examination the consensus recommends.
📊

Prevalence

2
Births registered by 15 European congenital anomaly registries, 2005-2006
Birth Prevalence 2.2 per 100,000 1–9 per 100,000 (births)
The start of the EUROCAT series: 0.22 per 10,000 births, which is 2.2 per 100,000. Recorded as its own period rather than as one end of a range, because the spread between this figure and the 2013-2014 one is a decline over time and not uncertainty about a single rate. This is the rate of registered diagnoses, not of the condition, and it underestimates it: a syndrome diagnosis requires that an anomaly registry recognise the pattern and record the exposure, and 17 of the 28 cases in the whole series came from one registry, which is more plausibly ascertainment than incidence. The quantity that tracks the true burden is the roughly 10% malformation risk among valproate-exposed pregnancies multiplied by prescribing in pregnancy.
Show evidence (3 references)
PMID:29753923 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Data from 15 European congenital anomaly registries, who are members of EUROCAT (A European network of population-based registries for the epidemiologic surveillance of congenital anomalies), identified 28 cases of valproate syndrome in 2.74 million births from 2005 to 2014."
The numerator and denominator this record normalizes.
PMID:29753923 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The prevalence of valproate syndrome in Europe significantly decreased from 0.22 per 10,000 births in 2005/6 to 0.03 per 10,000 births in 2013/14."
The decline the rate bounds represent, and the reason a single central figure would misdescribe this record.
PMID:29753923 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"One registry, Ile de la Reunion, had the majority of cases (17)."
The ascertainment concentration behind the caution that this is a registered-diagnosis rate.
Births registered by 15 European congenital anomaly registries, 2013-2014
Birth Prevalence 0.3 per 100,000 1–9 per 1,000,000 (births)
The end of the same EUROCAT series: 0.03 per 10,000 births, which is 0.3 per 100,000, a significant decline from 2005-2006. The same registered-diagnosis caveat applies.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:29753923 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"The prevalence of valproate syndrome in Europe significantly decreased from 0.22 per 10,000 births in 2005/6 to 0.03 per 10,000 births in 2013/14."
The later rate, and the statement that the decline is significant.
PMID:29753923 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"After excluding these cases there still remained a decreasing trend even though it no longer reached statistical significance due to the small number of cases."
Bounds the decline: excluding the one dominant registry leaves the trend in the same direction but no longer significant.
⚖️

Clinical Burden

High
Lifelong cognitive and social disability in a condition wholly preventable by not exposing the fetus. Among individuals carrying the diagnosis, moderate or severe cognitive impairment affects over half and three quarters need formal educational support; the structural malformations add surgical morbidity on top of that. The burden is a prescribing burden as much as a biological one.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:37666366 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"Individuals with a diagnosis of FVSD showed significantly higher levels of moderate (43.4%) and severe (14.4%) cognitive impairment than other groups (p = 0.003), high levels of required formal educational support (77.6%), and poorer academic competence than individuals not exposed to Valproate..."
Quantifies the disability and the support requirement in a cohort defined by having the diagnosis.
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"Though we expect the number of individuals with valproate related difficulties to decline in future years due to changes in prescribing"
The consensus group's own framing of the burden as one prescribing change reduces, which is why it is recorded as preventable rather than only severe.
🔀

Differential Diagnoses

3

Conditions with similar clinical presentations that must be differentiated from Fetal Valproate Syndrome:

Fetal alcohol spectrum disorder MONDO:0000408
Overlapping Features The other common teratogenic cause of facial dysmorphism with neurodevelopmental impairment. The lip and philtrum features overlap directly, and both conditions depend on exposure history rather than on a test, so a candid exposure history is what separates them.
Distinguishing Features
  • Short palpebral fissures and the smooth philtrum plus thin upper lip triad characterize fetal alcohol spectrum disorder; the valproate gestalt adds an everted lower lip, metopic ridging and arched eyebrows, and carries a specific malformation set — spina bifida, isolated cleft palate, radial ray defects — that fetal alcohol spectrum disorder does not.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:31324220 SUPPORT Other
"The facial dysmorphism is characterised by a broad nasal bridge, short nose with forward-facing (anteverted) nostrils, small mouth with thin upper lip, everted lower lip flat philtrum, ridging of the metopic suture and neatly arched eyebrows"
The valproate gestalt against which the alcohol one is compared. The comparison itself is this entry's, not the source's.
Chromatinopathies
Overlapping Features Mendelian disorders of the epigenetic machinery, Kleefstra syndrome among them. The overlap is not coincidental: valproate inhibits histone deacetylases, and these disorders disrupt the same chromatin regulation genetically, so the phenotypic resemblance may reflect a shared perturbed mechanism. This is the differential that makes the mandatory genetic testing worth doing rather than defensive.
Distinguishing Features
  • A causative variant on sequencing, and absence of documented valproate exposure. The valproate episignature is a candidate discriminator since several chromatinopathies have their own episignatures.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:33959609 SUPPORT Other
"Critically examining the phenotype of FVSD and chromatinopathies, they shared several overlapping features that can be observed despite the different etiologies of these disorders, suggesting the possible existence of a common perturbed mechanism(s) during embryonic development."
States both the phenotypic overlap and the mechanistic reason for it, which is why this differential is curated with the mechanism rather than only as a look-alike.
Fetal carbamazepine syndrome Not Yet Curated MONDO:0018263
Overlapping Features The other antiseizure medication embryofetopathy. Both belong to the older category of fetal anticonvulsant syndrome, and the clinic series that describe the phenotype often mix them.
Distinguishing Features
  • Carbamazepine carries about half the malformation rate of valproate (5.4% against 9.9% in EURAP) and, unlike valproate, is not associated with reduced IQ or with autism in the prospective cohorts. Exposure history separates them.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:38497990 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"MCMs occurred in 153 of 1549 pregnancies for valproate (9.9%; 95% CI, 8.5%-11.5%), 9 of 142 for phenytoin (6.3%; 95% CI, 3.4%-11.6%), 21 of 338 for phenobarbital (6.2%; 95% CI, 4.1%-9.3%), 121 of 2255 for carbamazepine (5.4%; 95% CI, 4.5%-6.4%), 10 of 204 for topiramate (4.9%; 95% CI,..."
The malformation rates by drug, which is the quantitative basis for separating valproate from the other antiseizure medication embryopathies.
PMID:23352199 SUPPORT Human Clinical
"age-6 IQ was lower after exposure to valproate (mean 97, 95% CI 94-101) than to carbamazepine"
The cognitive separation between the two exposures in a prospective cohort.
🧫

Experimental Models

5
Genotyped human neural progenitor population exposed to valproic acid PRIMARY_CELL_CULTURE
A population rather than a cell line, which is the point: with 83 genotyped donors the design can separate what the drug does in everyone from what it does only in some genetic backgrounds. Valproate remodelled two-thirds of measured chromatin peaks, reduced proliferation in every donor at a clinically relevant concentration, and shifted cells toward differentiation. Genetic variation modified the response at over a thousand loci, and a transcriptome-wide association in the valproate condition implicated folate metabolism genes in cognitive ability — which lands the two mechanistic branches curated here, chromatin and folate, in one human experiment.
neural progenitor cell CL:0011020 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this experimental model uses this cell type This experimental model uses neural progenitor cell (CL:0011020). CL:0011020 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
Organism

Cell source
Primary human neural progenitor cells from 83 genotyped multi-ancestry donors
Culture
Monolayer, 48-hour exposure to 1 mM valproic acid
Publication
PMID:41935183
Pathograph links
Embryonic Histone Hyperacetylation and Ectopic Transcription measures fidelity: moderate observes: molecular Measures the chromatin response directly in human cells, and finds it is not the simple opening that HDAC inhibition alone would predict.
Limitations: A single cell type and a 48-hour acute exposure, which the authors state does not model chronic clinical administration. Chromatin accessibility is not histone acetylation, so the mark itself is inferred rather than measured.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Our use of hNPCs, while highly relevant for studying the teratogenic effects of prenatal VPA exposure and the proliferative impacts of Li, represents only a single cell type [5, 21]."
The authors' own statement of the first limitation.
+ 1 more reference
Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular The proliferation reduction is present in every donor at 1 mM, so it is a property of the exposure rather than of a susceptible subset.
Limitations: Proliferation in dissociated culture is not neurogenesis in a developing cortex, and the direction conflicts with what mouse cortex shows in vivo.
Readouts

Direction: DECREASED
Interpretation: Consistent across all donors at a clinically relevant concentration, with near-complete arrest at the highest concentrations.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"VPA exposure leads to consistent reductions in proliferation across all donors at clinically relevant concentrations"
The measured readout and its consistency across the donor panel.
Show evidence (2 references)
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Here, we applied psychiatric and neurological disorder treatments to a genotyped population of human neural progenitors (n = 83 donors) and measured molecular responses on chromatin accessibility and gene expression."
The design, which is what distinguishes this from a single-line exposure study.
PMID:41935183 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Using a transcriptome-wide association study (TWAS), we found that the effects of VPA on cognitive ability are mediated by both environmental and genetic effects on the folate metabolism pathway."
Connects the exposure's molecular effect to cognitive ability through the folate pathway, which is the branch the clinical prevention advice targets.
Human spinal cord organoid model of valproate-induced neurulation failure ORGANOID
Organoids that recapitulate aspects of human neurulation and fail to do so under valproate, with cell-cell junction genes and proteins the main thing that changes. The result is cross-validated in mouse embryos showing the same gene changes and the same defect, which is what makes it more than an in-vitro curiosity.

Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular Identifies the junctional programme as the thing that changes, in human tissue, during the process that fails.
Limitations: Organoids lack the surrounding mesoderm, surface ectoderm and mechanical environment that contribute to closure in vivo, and no axial patterning establishes that the lumbosacral level is the one modelled.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37643760 SUPPORT In Vitro
"Collectively, our data demonstrate that hSCOs provide a valuable biological resource for dissecting the molecular pathways underlying the currently unknown human neurulation process using destructive biological analysis tools."
The authors' framing of what the model is for and what it can establish.
Human single-rosette brain organoid model of apical constriction failure ORGANOID
Self-organizing single-rosette spheroids in which valproate enlarges the apical lumen by reducing apical constriction, the same cell behaviour affected by loss of SHROOM3. The convergence of a teratogen and a known genetic cause of neural tube defects on one cell behaviour is what the model contributes. Registered here because its findings are cited as evidence on two mechanism nodes and one edge in this entry; the Anencephaly entry uses the same system.
Organism
human NCBITaxon:9606 NCBI Taxonomy (NCBITaxon) Relation: this experimental model is built in this organism This experimental model is built in human, annotated with Homo sapiens (NCBITaxon:9606). NCBITaxon:9606 is an organism from the NCBI Taxonomy.
Cell source
Human pluripotent stem cell-derived self-organizing single-rosette spheroids
Publication
PMID:37443734
Pathograph links
Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity measures fidelity: moderate observes: cellular Measures apical constriction directly, by lumen size, and shows a teratogenic and a genetic cause of neural tube defects converging on it.
Limitations: The spheroids have no dorsoventral or rostrocaudal patterning, so the model cannot speak to the axial level at which the human defect occurs — which is the central limitation for this disease, whose characteristic lesion is lumbosacral. Lumen size is a proxy for a cell behaviour rather than a measurement of closure: no neural tube either closes or fails to close in this system.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:37443734 SUPPORT In Vitro
"The increased lumen sizes were caused by reduced cell apical constriction suggesting that impingement of this process is a shared mechanism for VPA treatment and SHROOM3-KO, two well-known causes of NTDs."
The measured readout and the convergence claim, stated with the authors' own hedge intact.
Human dorsal forebrain organoid model of outer radial glia depletion ORGANOID
Exposure reduces organoid size and progenitor expansion dose-dependently, and specifically depletes outer radial glia — the progenitor subtype responsible for the expansion of the human neocortex, and one that rodent models represent poorly. The transcriptional changes overlap genes dysregulated in autistic brains and organoids.
radial glial cell CL:0000681 Cell Ontology (CL) Relation: this experimental model uses this cell type This experimental model uses radial glial cell (CL:0000681). CL:0000681 is a cell type from the Cell Ontology.
