human NCBITaxon:9606 NCBI Taxonomy (NCBITaxon) Relation: this experimental model is built in this organism This experimental model is built in human, annotated with Homo sapiens (NCBITaxon:9606). NCBITaxon:9606 is an organism from the NCBI Taxonomy.

Limitations: Organoids are immature, lack vasculature, microglia and long-range inputs, and have no behavioural readout. The Wnt/beta-catenin involvement reported here is an association within the model, not a demonstrated requirement in vivo.

The overlap with autism transcriptional signatures, which is the link from this model to the clinical phenotype.
Directly reprogrammed human neurons with staged valproate exposure IPSC_DERIVED_MODEL
The design answers a question the clinical data pose but cannot settle: why exposure after organogenesis still harms neurodevelopment. By exposing human neurons at defined maturation stages it shows the damage is restricted to immature neurons, and identifies MARCKSL1 as the mediator downstream of HDAC and GSK-3 inhibition.

Limitations: Directly reprogrammed neurons mature on an accelerated, artificial schedule that may not map onto fetal timing, and single cultured neurons lack the circuit context in which dendritic deficits would produce a phenotype.

Animal Models

4
Prenatal valproate rat model of autism Teratogen exposure model
A single dose around embryonic day 12.5 produces reduced social behaviour, stereotypy, altered sensory thresholds and delayed maturation. This is the most-used environmental model of autism in neuroscience, which cuts both ways here: the behavioural validation is unusually thorough, and almost all of it was done to model idiopathic autism rather than this syndrome.
Species
Rattus norvegicus
Genotype
Wild type
Background
Wistar and Sprague-Dawley
Publication
PMID:15238991
Pathograph links
Neurodevelopmental Impairment partially recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: organism Reproduces the behavioural end of the neurodevelopmental arm, including the sensory abnormalities that the human cohort data put at 80% of diagnosed individuals. Pointed at the organism-scale outcome node rather than at circuit formation, because what this model observes is behaviour.
Limitations: A single high intraperitoneal bolus at one developmental moment is not the continuous, variable oral exposure of a human pregnancy, and the model was built and validated against idiopathic autism rather than against fetal valproate syndrome. It says nothing about the malformation arm, and the behaviours scored are rodent analogues of human constructs.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:15238991 SUPPORT Model Organism
"The results of the present experiments demonstrate that VPA rats exhibit: (1) lower sensitivity to pain and higher sensitivity to nonpainful stimuli, (2) diminished acoustic prepulse inhibition, (3) locomotor and repetitive/stereotypic-like hyperactivity combined with lower exploratory activity,..."
The behavioural battery that defines what this model recapitulates.
Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: tissue The same exposure and timing reduce Purkinje cell number in the cerebellar vermis, with the posterior lobe affected more than the anterior — a regional gradient rather than a global loss.
Limitations: The human comparison is autism neuropathology, not neuropathology from individuals with fetal valproate syndrome, so the parallel is to a related condition.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:10840175 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Within the vermis, the reduction in volume was significantly greater in the posterior lobe than in the anterior lobe."
The regional specificity, which is what makes this more than a size difference.
Mthfr 677C>T knock-in mouse with valproate exposure Gene-environment interaction model
The experimental counterpart of the human maternal-genotype association: mice carrying the common MTHFR variant malform more after the same valproate dose than wild-type littermates, and show the expected biochemistry — lower enzyme activity and raised homocysteine.
Species
Mus musculus
Genotype
Mthfr677C>T knock-in, CC, CT and TT
Genes
MTHFR hgnc:7436 HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee (hgnc) Relation: this experimental model concerns this gene This experimental model concerns MTHFR (hgnc:7436). hgnc:7436 is a gene from the HUGO Gene Nomenclature Committee.
Publication
PMID:40645457
Pathograph links
Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism perturbs fidelity: moderate observes: organism Tests the folate branch by varying one-carbon capacity genetically instead of varying the drug, which is the more informative direction.
Limitations: A single 400 mg/kg intraperitoneal dose at E7.5, and the readout is malformation rate rather than a measurement of folate status in the embryo. The human association is with the mother's genotype; in the mouse, dam and fetus share it, so the experiment cannot separate maternal from fetal contribution.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:40645457 SUPPORT Model Organism
"Consistent with the clinical data, Mthfr677C>T mice also showed lower liver MTHFR enzymatic activity and elevated serum homocysteine (Hcy) levels."
Confirms the engineered allele does what it should before the exposure is applied.
Mouse neural tube defect model with NRF2 induction Teratogen exposure model with pharmacological rescue
Valproate exposure produces open neural tubes and a transiently oxidising glutathione redox potential; pretreatment with an NRF2 inducer prevents both. The rescue is what makes the redox branch a candidate mechanism rather than a correlate.
Species
Mus musculus
Genotype
Wild type
Background
CD-1 and C57BL/6J
Publication
PMID:34737154
Pathograph links
Embryonic Redox Disruption rescues fidelity: moderate observes: cellular Intervening on the antioxidant response, not on the drug, reduces both the redox shift and the defect.
Limitations: The mouse defect is exencephaly, a cranial failure, while the characteristic human lesion is lumbosacral spina bifida, so the axial level differs. D3T induces a broad transcriptional programme, so the rescue is not specific to glutathione redox state. The two halves of the result also come from different preparations: the redox measurement from CD-1 whole-embryo culture with valproate applied in vitro, and the defect rescue from C57BL/6J dams dosed in utero, so no single experiment shows both in one strain and system.
Show evidence (1 reference)
PMID:34737154 SUPPORT Model Organism
"These data show that NRF2 induction via D3T pretreatment protects against VPA-induced redox dysregulation and decreases the prevalence of NTDs in developing mouse embryos."
The authors' statement of the rescue in both the mechanism and the outcome.
Rat cranial neural crest exposure model Stage-specific teratogen exposure model
A single dose immediately before cranial crest formation, read out by whole-mount in situ hybridization and immunohistochemistry for crest markers. The design is what distinguishes it: dosing is timed to the developmental event rather than to a convenient day, so the result is stage-specific.
Species
Rattus norvegicus
Genotype
Wild type
Publication

Pathograph links
Disturbed Cranial Neural Crest Formation and Migration recapitulates fidelity: moderate observes: cellular Shows both halves of the node — reduced crest formation at midbrain-rhombomere 1/2 and misrouted rhombomere 4 crest carrying the wrong positional identity.
Limitations: Embryonic readouts over hours to days, with no follow-through to a formed face, so the connection to the human gestalt is inferred. Rat and human craniofacial development differ in the relative contribution of the frontonasal mass.

An independent verification of the migration abnormality in cultured embryos.

Source YAML

click to show 
name: Fetal Valproate Syndrome
creation_date: '2026-10-05T16:00:00Z'
description: >-
  Fetal valproate syndrome is the pattern of congenital malformation, facial
  dysmorphism and neurodevelopmental impairment that follows exposure to valproic
  acid before birth. Maternal valproate therapy is the sole necessary cause, and
  the effect is dose-dependent: major malformations occur in about 10% of
  monotherapy-exposed pregnancies overall and about a quarter above 1500 mg
  daily. Three candidate upstream lesions are curated here in parallel rather
  than ranked — inhibition of class I histone deacetylases, antagonism of folate
  receptors and one-carbon metabolism, and a transient oxidising shift in
  embryonic redox state — because each has its own rescue or structure-activity
  evidence and none accounts for the whole phenotype. The 2019 European
  Reference Network consensus renamed the condition fetal valproate spectrum
  disorder to capture its range, and this entry treats the neurodevelopmental arm
  as the larger one: cognitive and social impairment is commoner than structural
  malformation and occurs without it.
category: Environmental
parents:
- Teratogenic disorder
- Neurodevelopmental Disorder
synonyms:
- FVS
- FVSD
- fetal valproic acid syndrome
- fetal valproate spectrum disorder
- valproic acid embryopathy
- valproate embryopathy
- susceptibility to valproate embryopathy
disease_term:
  preferred_term: fetal valproate syndrome
  term:
    id: MONDO:0012275
    label: fetal valproate syndrome

prevalence:
- population: Births registered by 15 European congenital anomaly registries, 2005-2006
  measure_type: BIRTH_PREVALENCE
  prevalence_class: BAND_1_9_PER_100000
  rate_per_100000: 2.2
  notes: >-
    The start of the EUROCAT series: 0.22 per 10,000 births, which is 2.2 per
    100,000. Recorded as its own period rather than as one end of a range, because
    the spread between this figure and the 2013-2014 one is a decline over time and
    not uncertainty about a single rate.

    This is the rate of registered diagnoses, not of the condition, and it
    underestimates it: a syndrome diagnosis requires that an anomaly registry
    recognise the pattern and record the exposure, and 17 of the 28 cases in the
    whole series came from one registry, which is more plausibly ascertainment than
    incidence. The quantity that tracks the true burden is the roughly 10%
    malformation risk among valproate-exposed pregnancies multiplied by prescribing
    in pregnancy.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:29753923
    reference_title: "Prevalence of valproate syndrome in Europe from 2005 to 2014: A registry based multi-centre study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Data from 15 European congenital anomaly registries, who are members of
      EUROCAT (A European network of population-based registries for the
      epidemiologic surveillance of congenital anomalies), identified 28 cases of
      valproate syndrome in 2.74 million births from 2005 to 2014.
    explanation: The numerator and denominator this record normalizes.
  - reference: PMID:29753923
    reference_title: "Prevalence of valproate syndrome in Europe from 2005 to 2014: A registry based multi-centre study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The prevalence of valproate syndrome in Europe significantly decreased from
      0.22 per 10,000 births in 2005/6 to 0.03 per 10,000 births in 2013/14.
    explanation: >-
      The decline the rate bounds represent, and the reason a single central
      figure would misdescribe this record.
  - reference: PMID:29753923
    reference_title: "Prevalence of valproate syndrome in Europe from 2005 to 2014: A registry based multi-centre study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "One registry, Ile de la Reunion, had the majority of cases (17)."
    explanation: >-
      The ascertainment concentration behind the caution that this is a
      registered-diagnosis rate.

clinical_burden:
  burden_level: HIGH
  rationale: >-
    Lifelong cognitive and social disability in a condition wholly preventable by
    not exposing the fetus. Among individuals carrying the diagnosis, moderate or
    severe cognitive impairment affects over half and three quarters need formal
    educational support; the structural malformations add surgical morbidity on
    top of that. The burden is a prescribing burden as much as a biological one.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:37666366
    reference_title: "Neurodevelopmental outcomes in children and adults with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder: A contribution from the ConcePTION project."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Individuals with a diagnosis of FVSD showed significantly higher levels of
      moderate (43.4%) and severe (14.4%) cognitive impairment than other groups
      (p = 0.003), high levels of required formal educational support (77.6%), and
      poorer academic competence than individuals not exposed to Valproate (p =
      0.001).
    explanation: >-
      Quantifies the disability and the support requirement in a cohort defined by
      having the diagnosis.
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Though we expect the number of individuals with valproate related
      difficulties to decline in future years due to changes in prescribing
    explanation: >-
      The consensus group's own framing of the burden as one prescribing change
      reduces, which is why it is recorded as preventable rather than only severe.
environmental:
- name: Maternal valproate therapy during pregnancy
  description: >-
    Valproic acid and its salts, taken for epilepsy, bipolar disorder or migraine
    prophylaxis, reaching the embryo by placental transfer. This is the sole
    necessary cause: the syndrome does not occur without it. Two features matter
    for the rest of the entry. It is dose-dependent, with no identified risk-free
    dose. And it is not explained by the maternal illness, because malformations
    follow valproate taken for indications other than epilepsy — which is what
    licenses treating the drug rather than the epilepsy as the cause.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: gestational maternal valproate exposure
    term:
      id: XCO:0001598
      label: gestational maternal exposure to valproate
  chemicals:
  - valproic acid
  - sodium valproate
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
    environmental_effect: TRIGGERS
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Maternal dosing delivers the drug to the embryo directly, at fetal
      concentrations exceeding maternal ones, which is the initiating step of
      every branch curated here.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:38203562
      reference_title: "Valproic Acid in Pregnancy Revisited: Neurobehavioral, Biochemical and Molecular Changes Affecting the Embryo and Fetus in Humans and in Animals: A Narrative Review."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: OTHER
      snippet: "VPA crosses the human placenta and was found at higher concentrations in fetal blood."
      explanation: >-
        States the transfer and the concentration gradient that make the embryo the
        exposed compartment. Source is OTHER because the citation is a narrative
        review reporting the finding, not a primary pharmacokinetic study.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      A pattern of major and minor congenital anomalies, facial dysmorphic
      features, and neurodevelopmental difficulties, including cognitive and
      social impairments has been reported in some children exposed to sodium
      valproate (VPA) during pregnancy.
    explanation: >-
      The consensus statement's definition of the condition as the consequence of
      this exposure.
  - reference: PMID:38203562
    reference_title: "Valproic Acid in Pregnancy Revisited: Neurobehavioral, Biochemical and Molecular Changes Affecting the Embryo and Fetus in Humans and in Animals: A Narrative Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      It induces malformations even when taken for indications other than
      epilepsy, adding to the data that epilepsy is not responsible for the
      teratogenic effects.
    explanation: >-
      Separates the drug from the maternal illness, which is the confounder that
      would otherwise explain the association.
  - reference: PMID:38497990
    reference_title: "Risk of Major Congenital Malformations and Exposure to Antiseizure Medication Monotherapy."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      For valproate, phenobarbital, and carbamazepine, there was a significant
      increase in the prevalence of MCMs associated with increasing dose of the
      ASM.
    explanation: Establishes dose dependence in the largest prospective registry.
  - reference: PMID:26085607
    reference_title: "Dose-dependent teratogenicity of valproate in mono- and polytherapy: an observational study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Regardless of exposure group, the frequency of MCMs increased with dose of
      VPA, being highest at doses ≥1,500 mg/d (24.0% for monotherapy, 31.0% for
      VPA + LTG, and 19.2% for VPA + other AEDs), and was similar across treatment
      groups at the lowest VPA dose level of <700 mg/d (5.9% for monotherapy, 7.0%
      for VPA + LTG, and 5.4% for VPA + other AEDs).
    explanation: >-
      The dose-response across the clinical range, including that risk at the
      lowest dose band is still above background.
  notes: >-
    The exposure term is XCO:0001598, the same binding the Autism_Spectrum_Disorder
    and Anencephaly entries use, so those three records can be queried together.
    Spina_Bifida_Cystica will not be found by that query: it binds ECTO:9001740
    (exposure to anticonvulsant), a drug-class term rather than a valproate one,
    even though it is the entry most directly about the valproate neural tube
    signal. ECTO has no gestational maternal valproate exposure
    term: it carries ECTO:9002607 (exposure to sodium valproate) and
    ECTO:0900506 (maternal exposure to valproate semisodium), neither of which is
    maternal gestational exposure to valproate itself.
- name: Periconceptional high-dose folic acid supplementation
  description: >-
    Folic acid taken before conception and through early pregnancy. Recorded as
    protective with its own contradiction attached, because the evidence
    separates by outcome and by timing. Across antiseizure medications, high-dose
    folic acid started in the twelve weeks before pregnancy reduced malformations
    while the same supplement started after pregnancy onset did not. For spina
    bifida specifically in valproate-exposed pregnancies, the protective effect
    seen in unexposed pregnancies was not detected, on an interval too wide to
    exclude one. The consensus statement calls the
    evidence for preventing valproate teratogenesis conflicting, and this entry
    records it that way rather than presenting folate as a solution.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: exposure to folic acid
    term:
      id: ECTO:9000123
      label: exposure to folic acid
  chemicals:
  - folic acid
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism
    environmental_effect: PROTECTS_AGAINST
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Supplementation is directed at the folate branch, and the animal rescue
      experiments act on it, but the human evidence does not establish that the
      observed protection runs through this node rather than through
      folate-dependent processes generally. Attached to this node rather than to
      the shared downstream outcome because it is the branch the intervention
      targets.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:3110521
      reference_title: "Valproic acid-induced neural tube defects: reduction by folinic acid in the mouse."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        Folinic acid (5-CHO-THF) coadministration by i.p. injection or by a
        constant rate infusion via osmotic minipumps, implanted s.c.,
        significantly reduced the exencephaly rates using a randomized
        double-blind experimental procedure.
      explanation: >-
        A blinded rescue in the mouse, the strongest evidence that supplying
        reduced folate counteracts the exposure.
    - reference: PMID:20680999
      reference_title: "Does folic acid use decrease the risk for spina bifida after in utero exposure to valproic acid?"
      supports: REFUTE
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        The ORs for the effect of correct folic acid use were calculated among
        antiepileptic drug (AED) unexposed pregnancies 0.5 [95%CI: 0.3-0.7] and
        among VPA exposed pregnancies 1.0 [95%CI: 0.1-7.6].
      explanation: >-
        The protective effect present in unexposed pregnancies is absent in
        valproate-exposed ones for spina bifida. Carried as a refutation rather
        than omitted, though the interval is wide enough that the study itself
        declines to call it a null result.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:41876129
    reference_title: "Timing of high-dose folic acid supplementation in the periconceptional period among women taking antiseizure medications and risk of major congenital anomalies: a target trial emulation."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Initiation of high-dose folic acid in the 12 weeks before pregnancy was
      associated with a reduced risk of MCA in offspring of women using ASMs while
      initiation in the 12 weeks after pregnancy onset was not.
    explanation: >-
      The timing contrast, in a target-trial emulation across antiseizure
      medications rather than valproate alone.
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "The evidence of high dose folic acid in preventing VPA teratogenesis is conflicting"
    explanation: >-
      The consensus group's own verdict, which is why this exposure carries
      evidence in both directions.
  notes: >-
    The folic acid evidence is not valproate-specific where it is strongest
    (PMID:41876129 pools antiseizure medications) and not protective where it is
    valproate-specific (PMID:20680999, spina bifida). A third result bears on the
    neurodevelopmental arm rather than on malformations: among
    antiepileptic-exposed children, autistic traits were commoner when the mother
    had not supplemented (PMID:29279889).
- name: Antiseizure medication polytherapy
  description: >-
    Valproate combined with another antiseizure medication. Recorded as modulating
    rather than exacerbating, because the EURAP analysis found the malformation rate
    tracked valproate dose rather than the combination: polytherapy marks higher risk
    without being shown to add risk of its own, and nothing cited claims co-medication
    increases placental valproate transfer.
  exposure_term:
    preferred_term: anticonvulsant exposure during pregnancy
    term:
      id: ECTO:9001740
      label: exposure to anticonvulsant
  influences_mechanisms:
  - target: Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
    environmental_effect: MODULATES
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Co-medication raises the observed malformation rate, but the same analysis
      attributes the increase primarily to valproate dose, so no mechanism is
      asserted for the combination itself.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:26085607
      reference_title: "Dose-dependent teratogenicity of valproate in mono- and polytherapy: an observational study."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        The risk of MCMs associated with VPA exposure increases with increasing
        VPA dose, both in the presence and in the absence of one concomitant AED,
        and appears to be related primarily to the dose of VPA.
      explanation: >-
        Supports the exposure being graded by valproate dose and simultaneously
        limits what polytherapy explains.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:26085607
    reference_title: "Dose-dependent teratogenicity of valproate in mono- and polytherapy: an observational study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The frequency of MCMs at 1 year after birth was 10.0% for VPA monotherapy,
      11.3% for exposures to VPA and LTG, and 11.7% for exposures to VPA +
      another (non-LTG) AED.
    explanation: >-
      The rates by combination, higher than monotherapy but close enough that the
      study attributes them to dose.
pathophysiology:
- name: Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  description: >-
    Valproate in the maternal circulation crosses the placenta and reaches the
    embryo at concentrations above the maternal ones. This node exists so that
    the three candidate upstream lesions below have a shared parent: they are
    branches of one exposure, not three separate diseases, and the entry does not
    rank them.
  chemical_entities:
  - preferred_term: valproic acid
    term:
      id: CHEBI:39867
      label: valproic acid
  locations:
  - preferred_term: embryo
    term:
      id: UBERON:0000922
      label: embryo
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38203562
    reference_title: "Valproic Acid in Pregnancy Revisited: Neurobehavioral, Biochemical and Molecular Changes Affecting the Embryo and Fetus in Humans and in Animals: A Narrative Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "VPA crosses the human placenta and was found at higher concentrations in fetal blood."
    explanation: >-
      Establishes that the embryo is exposed, and at what relative concentration.
      Source is OTHER because the citation is a narrative review.
  - reference: PMID:38203562
    reference_title: "Valproic Acid in Pregnancy Revisited: Neurobehavioral, Biochemical and Molecular Changes Affecting the Embryo and Fetus in Humans and in Animals: A Narrative Review."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Several teratogenic mechanisms are proposed for VPA, but the most important
      mechanisms seem to be its effects on the metabolism of folate, SAMe and
      histones, thus affecting DNA methylation.
    explanation: >-
      A review naming the same branches this node feeds, and recording that they
      are proposals rather than a settled single mechanism.
  downstream:
  - target: Class I Histone Deacetylase Inhibition
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Valproate inhibits HDAC1 at concentrations in the therapeutic range, so the
      enzyme is engaged directly by the drug that reaches the embryo.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:11473107
      reference_title: "Histone deacetylase is a direct target of valproic acid, a potent anticonvulsant, mood stabilizer, and teratogen."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        Valproic acid, however, acts through a distinct pathway that involves
        direct inhibition of histone deacetylase (IC(50) for HDAC1 = 0.4 mm).
      explanation: The direct enzyme inhibition, with the potency that makes it plausible in vivo.
  - target: Folate Receptor Antagonism
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Valproate binds the high-affinity folate receptors noncompetitively, which is a
      second drug-target interaction alongside the HDAC one. Calling the two
      branches parallel is a statement about how they are curated, not a claim that
      they are independent: valproate also lowers placental expression of folate
      carriers and one-carbon enzymes, which would make the folate lesion partly
      downstream of the transcriptional one. Both routes are recorded here and
      neither is ranked.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:25066307
      reference_title: Brief report novel mechanism for valproate-induced teratogenicity.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        Binding affinities experimentally determined through enzyme-linked
        immunosorbent assay assays indicate that VPA serves as a noncompetitive
        substrate that can lessen the ability of the three primary folate forms to
        bind to the high affinity folate receptors.
      explanation: The measured binding interaction that opens this branch.
    - reference: PMID:34293696
      reference_title: "Effects of valproate, an HDAC inhibitor, on the expression of folate carriers and folate metabolism-related genes in the placenta of rats."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        Repeated VPA administration reduced the placental expression of Folr1and Mtr
        on GD20 and increased the expression of Dhfr on GD13 compared with the
        control.
      explanation: >-
        A second route onto this branch, and the reason the description does not call
        it independent of the HDAC one: valproate changes the expression of folate
        carriers and one-carbon enzymes, which is a transcriptional effect on folate
        handling rather than a direct receptor interaction. The cached text runs
        "Folr1and" together, and is quoted as cached.
  - target: Embryonic Redox Disruption
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Exposed embryos shift transiently to a more oxidising glutathione redox
      potential. Marked indirect because no step is established between the drug
      and the redox change itself.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:34737154
      reference_title: NRF2 activation inhibits valproic acid-induced neural tube defects in mice.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        Embryos treated with VPA exhibited a transiently oxidizing GSH/GSSG Eh,
        while those that received D3T pretreatment prior to VPA exposure showed no
        differences compared to controls.
      explanation: The measured redox shift that this edge asserts, with its pharmacological control.
- name: Class I Histone Deacetylase Inhibition
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Valproate inhibits class I histone deacetylases, with HDAC1 inhibited at
    about 0.4 mM. The case that this is a teratogenic mechanism and not merely a
    pharmacological property rests on structure-activity evidence rather than on
    correlation: across twenty valproate derivatives, HDAC inhibitory potency
    tracks teratogenic potency, non-teratogenic analogues do not inhibit the
    enzyme, and a structurally unrelated HDAC inhibitor reproduces the
    malformations.
  genes:
  - preferred_term: HDAC1
    term:
      id: hgnc:4852
      label: HDAC1
  molecular_functions:
  - preferred_term: histone deacetylase activity
    modifier: DECREASED
    term:
      id: GO:0004407
      label: histone deacetylase activity
  chemical_entities:
  - preferred_term: valproic acid
    term:
      id: CHEBI:39867
      label: valproic acid
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16485903
    reference_title: "Teratogenic effects mediated by inhibition of histone deacetylases: evidence from quantitative structure activity relationships of 20 valproic acid derivatives."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      We also show a quantitative correlation between the IC50(HDAC) and the
      teratogenic potential of VPA derivatives, which clearly points toward HDACs
      as the formerly described teratogenic receptors of VPA-induced neural tube
      defects (NTDs).
    explanation: >-
      The structure-activity argument: potency against the enzyme predicts
      teratogenic potency across a derivative series.
  - reference: PMID:16485903
    reference_title: "Teratogenic effects mediated by inhibition of histone deacetylases: evidence from quantitative structure activity relationships of 20 valproic acid derivatives."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      We demonstrate that only VPA derivatives with a teratogenic potential in
      mice are able to induce a hyperacetylation in core histone H4 in
      teratocarcinoma F9 cells.
    explanation: >-
      The converse arm: non-teratogenic analogues fail to produce the mark.
  - reference: PMID:15901671
    reference_title: Association of valproate-induced teratogenesis with histone deacetylase inhibition in vivo.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      We found that VPA and other HDAC inhibitors cause very similar and
      characteristic developmental defects whereas VPA analogs with poor
      inhibitory activity in vivo have little teratogenic effect.
    explanation: >-
      The same dissociation in whole embryos, in two species, with an unrelated
      HDAC inhibitor as the positive comparator.
  downstream:
  - target: Embryonic Histone Hyperacetylation and Ectopic Transcription
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Removing deacetylase activity leaves histones hyperacetylated and derepresses
      transcription, which is the immediate consequence of the inhibition.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:11473107
      reference_title: "Histone deacetylase is a direct target of valproic acid, a potent anticonvulsant, mood stabilizer, and teratogen."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        At therapeutic levels, valproic acid mimics the histone deacetylase
        inhibitor trichostatin A, causing hyperacetylation of histones in cultured
        cells.
      explanation: The mark that follows the inhibition, at therapeutic drug levels.
- name: Embryonic Histone Hyperacetylation and Ectopic Transcription
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    In the exposed embryo the acetylation mark rises within hours and is not
    uniform: it is concentrated in the neuroepithelium, heart and somites, which
    are the tissues that go on to malform. Other histone marks move with it while
    global DNA methylation does not, so the proximate lesion is chromatin state
    rather than methylation. In human neural progenitors the drug reaches
    two-thirds of all measured chromatin peaks, and — against the expectation for
    an HDAC inhibitor — closes more regions than it opens.
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: epigenetic regulation of gene expression
    modifier: DYSREGULATED
    term:
      id: GO:0040029
      label: epigenetic regulation of gene expression
  - preferred_term: chromatin remodeling
    modifier: DYSREGULATED
    term:
      id: GO:0006338
      label: chromatin remodeling
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: neuroepithelial stem cell
    term:
      id: CL:0002259
      label: neuroepithelial stem cell
  locations:
  - preferred_term: developing neuroepithelium
    term:
      id: UBERON:0034705
      label: developing neuroepithelium
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20705080
    reference_title: Epigenetic modifications in valproic acid-induced teratogenesis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Immunohistochemical staining revealed increased histone acetylation in the
      neuroepithelium, heart, and somites.
    explanation: >-
      The tissue distribution of the mark, which matches the organs that malform
      and is why this node is not merely a cell-culture observation.
  - reference: PMID:20705080
    reference_title: Epigenetic modifications in valproic acid-induced teratogenesis.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      No significant differences in global or CpG island DNA methylation were
      observed in embryo homogenates.
    explanation: >-
      Bounds the claim: the measurable embryonic change is in histone marks, not
      in global DNA methylation.
  - reference: PMID:16193500

    explanation: >-

  downstream:
  - target: Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      In human neural progenitors the same exposure that remodels chromatin shifts
      the cells out of the cycle toward differentiation, with the closed regions
      enriched for proliferative transcription-factor motifs and the opened ones for
      neuronal differentiation. The cited experiment measures both changes under one
      exposure rather than showing that the first causes the second, and
      transcription necessarily intervenes, so the link is recorded as indirect with
      unknown intermediates.

      explanation: >-

- name: Folate Receptor Antagonism
  biological_scale: MOLECULAR
  description: >-
    Valproate binds the high-affinity folate receptors noncompetitively, so exposed
    cells take up less folate. This is the drug-target step of the folate branch,
    separated from the embryonic metabolic consequence below because the two are
    measured in different systems and at different scales: receptor binding in
    cells, metabolite pools in whole embryos.

- name: Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    In the exposed embryo the pattern of folate metabolites shifts, with
    formyl-tetrahydrofolates falling and tetrahydrofolate rising, attributed to
    inhibited transfer of the formyl group. The structure-activity argument applies
    on this branch as it does on the HDAC one: an analogue with antiepileptic
    activity but no teratogenic activity leaves embryonic folate metabolism alone.
    MTHFR is named here because the maternal 677C>T genotype modifies this branch's
    susceptibility, and the molecular function it reduces is what connects the gene
    to the metabolic disturbance: the knock-in mouse carrying that variant shows
    lower MTHFR enzymatic activity and raised homocysteine. The association and its
    limits are curated in the genetic section.

- name: Embryonic Redox Disruption
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Exposed embryos show a transiently oxidising glutathione redox potential and
    raised reactive oxygen species concentrated in the head region, with
    apoptosis tracking the oxidant signal. The branch is curated because it
    carries pharmacological rescue in two independent directions — an NRF2
    inducer given beforehand, and catalase in culture — rather than because the
    association is strong.

    supports: REFUTE

- name: Failed Neural Tube Closure

    one that first identified valproate as a teratogen.

- name: Disturbed Cranial Neural Crest Formation and Migration
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-

    frontonasal mass and first branchial arch, which is the anatomical basis for
    the facial gestalt and for the palate.

  - target: Trigonocephaly
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      The severe end of the metopic continuum, where the ridging has become suture
      fusion.
  - target: Craniosynostosis
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: Premature suture fusion, metopic in the reported cases.
  - target: Cleft palate
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Palatal shelf fusion fails. Curated as a consequence of this node because the
      affected crest streams build the first branchial arch, from which the palate
      derives.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:26408639
      reference_title: In utero exposure to valproate increases the risk of isolated cleft palate.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
      snippet: >-
        This distinction is important as these conditions have different management
        implications and the distinction has implications too for understanding the
        teratogenic mechanisms.
      explanation: >-
        The authors' point that which cleft type occurs bears on the mechanism, which
        is why this edge distinguishes isolated cleft palate rather than clefting
        generally.
- name: Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Exposure changes the balance between progenitor self-renewal and
    differentiation, and the direction of the change is not consistent across
    systems. Human neural progenitors from 83 donors and human forebrain organoids
    both lose proliferative capacity; rat cortical progenitors differentiate
    prematurely through a beta-catenin-Ras-ERK-p21 route; but mouse cortex in vivo
    shows the opposite, with progenitors failing to exit the cycle on schedule.
    The node is curated as a disturbance of the balance rather than as a direction,
    and the disagreement is carried in the attached evidence and in a discussion.
  biological_processes:
  - preferred_term: neural precursor cell proliferation
    modifier: DYSREGULATED
    term:
      id: GO:0061351
      label: neural precursor cell proliferation
  - preferred_term: neuron differentiation
    modifier: DYSREGULATED

      label: neuron differentiation
  - preferred_term: canonical Wnt signaling pathway
    modifier: DYSREGULATED
    term:
      id: GO:0060070

      id: CL:0011020
      label: neural progenitor cell

      id: CL:0000681
      label: radial glial cell
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:41935183
    reference_title: Assessing molecular gene by treatment interactions using a population of neural progenitors exposed to valproic acid and lithium.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      VPA exposure leads to consistent reductions in proliferation across all
      donors at clinically relevant concentrations
    explanation: >-
      The effect is present in every donor at a clinically relevant concentration,
      which is what makes this a property of the exposure rather than of a
      genotype.
  - reference: PMID:36523605
    reference_title: Valproic acid exposure decreases neurogenic potential of outer radial glia in human brain organoids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      We found that VPA exposure in organoids caused a reduction in the size and
      impairment in the proliferation and expansion of neural progenitor cells
      (NPCs) in a dose-dependent manner.
    explanation: >-
      The same direction in a three-dimensional human model, and dose-dependently.
  - reference: PMID:19068119
    reference_title: Valproic acid induces differentiation and inhibition of proliferation in neural progenitor cells via the beta-catenin-Ras-ERK-p21Cip/WAF1 pathway.
    supports: SUPPORT

      We report here that 1 mM VPA simultaneously induces differentiation and

      embryonic day 14 (E14) rat cerebral cortex neural progenitor cells (NPCs).
    explanation: >-
      Names the signalling route for the differentiation arm, at the same
      concentration as the human progenitor work.
  - reference: PMID:27798144
    reference_title: In Utero Exposure to Valproic Acid Induces Neocortical Dysgenesis via Dysregulation of Neural Progenitor Cell Proliferation/Differentiation.
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      We show that VPA decreases the probability of differentiation of the neural
      progenitor cells (NPCs) in mice, resulting in an abnormally increased number
      of projection neurons in the superficial layers of the neocortex.
    explanation: >-
      The opposite direction in vivo: progenitors differentiate less, not more.
      Carried as a refutation of the directional reading of this node, which is
      why the node asserts a disturbed balance instead.
  downstream:
  - target: Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis
    causal_link_type: DIRECT
    description: >-
      Whichever direction the balance shifts, the cortex is built from the wrong
      number of cells at the wrong time; the mouse work states the outcome as
      neocortical dysgenesis.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:27798144
      reference_title: In Utero Exposure to Valproic Acid Induces Neocortical Dysgenesis via Dysregulation of Neural Progenitor Cell Proliferation/Differentiation.

      snippet: >-
        Together, our findings show that VPA exposure in utero alters
        proliferation/differentiation characteristics of neural progenitor cells and

      explanation: The authors' own statement of this edge.
    - reference: PMID:36523605

      supports: SUPPORT

        cells (oRGs), a subtype of NPCs contributing to mammalian neocortical

      explanation: >-
        The human version of the same edge, through the progenitor subtype that
        expands the neocortex.
- name: Neuroepithelial Senescence
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    Exposed neuroepithelium induces cellular senescence, and genetic dissection

    negative claim: p19Arf accounts for the brain-growth deficit but not for

    only and is deliberately not connected to neural tube closure.

  notes: >-

    (PMID:38476755). See the fvs_microcephaly_mismatch discussion.
- name: Impaired Dendritic Morphogenesis and Synapse Maturation
  biological_scale: CELLULAR
  description: >-
    In human neurons, exposure impairs dendritic morphology and function through
    HDAC and GSK-3 inhibition with MARCKSL1 as a named mediator. The finding that
    matters clinically is the restriction: immature neurons are damaged and mature
    ones are not. That makes this the branch that explains why exposure after
    organogenesis still harms neurodevelopment, and why the neurodevelopmental
    phenotype extends beyond children with malformations.

- name: Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  description: >-
    The cortex is assembled with altered progenitor output: fewer outer radial
    glia in human organoids, excess superficial-layer projection neurons in mouse,
    and reduced Purkinje cell number in the rat cerebellum. The lamination and
    cell-number abnormalities are the tissue-level bridge between the progenitor
    disturbance and the behavioural outcome.

  downstream:
  - target: Altered Neural Circuit Formation
    causal_link_type: INDIRECT_UNKNOWN_INTERMEDIATES
    description: >-
      Building the cortex and cerebellum with the wrong cell numbers and laminar
      distribution is taken to alter the circuits they form. This edge carries no
      evidence of its own on purpose: no cited experiment follows a specific
      cytoarchitectural change through to a circuit measurement, and the one sentence
      that might have been used here — that the rat cerebellar findings parallel
      human autism neuropathology — is a cross-species validity remark about Purkinje
      counts, not a statement about this step. It is attached to the source node
      instead.
- name: Altered Neural Circuit Formation
  biological_scale: TISSUE
  description: >-
    Circuits assembled from disordered neurogenesis and from neurons whose dendrites
    and synapses matured abnormally. This node is the circuit-level step only; the
    clinical consequence is the separate organism-scale node below, because no cited
    experiment follows a circuit measurement through to a cognitive score and
    bundling the two would hide that gap.

      The step from altered circuits to measured cognition and behaviour. It carries

- name: Neurodevelopmental Impairment
  biological_scale: ORGANISM
  description: >-
    The clinical outcome of the neurodevelopmental arm: reduced IQ with a
    disproportionate verbal deficit, language impairment, autism and ADHD. The
    evidence here is epidemiological and dose-dependent rather than mechanistic,
    which is why it sits in its own node rather than inside the circuit-level one.

phenotypes:
- name: Cognitive impairment
  category: Neurologic
  frequency: FREQUENT
  diagnostic: true
  description: >-
    The defining feature of the spectrum and the commonest one. Group studies of
    exposed children put the IQ deficit at 8-10 points; in individuals carrying a
    clinical diagnosis of the syndrome it is about 19 points, with a
    characteristic verbal-comprehension shortfall. The consensus statement makes
    the point that cognitive impairment is commoner than malformation, which is
    why it is curated as the leading phenotype and not as a complication of the
    malformations.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Cognitive impairment
    term:

      label: Cognitive impairment

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: >-

      unexposed individuals and specific deficits in verbal skills
    explanation: The group-level deficit, described by the consensus as replicated.

    supports: SUPPORT

- name: Autism
  category: Behavioral
  frequency: OCCASIONAL
  description: >-
    Autism is the neurodevelopmental diagnosis most strongly associated with the
    exposure: 2.7% to 10.5% of exposed children in population cohorts, 6-15% of
    exposed individuals by the consensus statement's reckoning, a threefold pooled
    hazard overall and sixfold above 1 g/day, and 62.9% among individuals who
    already carry a clinical diagnosis. The frequency band follows the population
    denominator; the diagnosed-cohort figure is far higher because that cohort is
    selected on being affected. The presentation is reported to differ
    from idiopathic autism in having an even sex ratio and no regression.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Autism
    term:
      id: HP:0000717
      label: Autism

    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: >-
      The prevalence of autism spectrum disorder (ASD) is 6–15% in VPA exposed
      individuals
    explanation: >-
      The frequency among exposed individuals. The band follows the
      per-exposed-pregnancy denominator, where the population cohorts put autism at
      2.7-10.5%; the 62.9% figure in this phenotype's description is the proportion
      among individuals already carrying the clinical diagnosis, a different
      denominator that would give VERY_FREQUENT and is not what the band reports.

    supports: SUPPORT

      and ADHD; the risks for ASD were greater at doses ≥ 1000 mg/day.
    explanation: The pooled estimate across eight cohorts totalling six million children.
  - reference: PMID:40059353
    reference_title: "Gestational Exposure to Valproate and Autism Spectrum Disorder or Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder in Offspring: Systematic Review and Meta-Analysis."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Notably, anytime gestational exposure to high-dose valproate (> 1.0 to 1.1
      g/day) was associated with a substantially elevated risk of ASD (aHR, 6.32;
      95% CI, 3.12-12.80, N = 1,719,825).
    explanation: >-
      Dose dependence of the autism risk. The discordant-sibling analysis in the same
      paper addresses confounding by family; the monotherapy analysis addresses
      polytherapy rather than confounding by indication, since every exposed mother
      has the indication and that analysis has no active comparator.
  - reference: PMID:16108456
    reference_title: Characteristics of fetal anticonvulsant syndrome associated autistic disorder.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Fetal anticonvulsant syndrome associated autistic disorder is characterized by
      an even sex ratio, absence of regression or skill loss, and language delay in
      the absence of global delay.
    explanation: >-
      The phenotypic differences from idiopathic autism, which is why this is curated
      as a feature of the syndrome rather than only as an increased risk.
  - reference: PMID:35639399
    reference_title: Association of Prenatal Exposure to Antiseizure Medication With Risk of Autism and Intellectual Disability.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      In same-aged children of mothers with epilepsy exposed to topiramate and
      valproate monotherapy, 4.3% and 2.7%, respectively, had ASD, and 3.1% and 2.4%
      had ID.
    explanation: >-
      Population-level absolute risks from a Nordic cohort of 4.5 million children,
      within mothers with epilepsy. These are the figures the frequency band uses for
      the per-exposed-pregnancy denominator.
  - reference: PMID:38507750
    reference_title: "Risk of Autism after Prenatal Topiramate, Valproate, or Lamotrigine Exposure."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      With restriction to children born to mothers with epilepsy, the incidence was
      4.2% with no exposure to antiseizure medication (8815 children), 6.2% with
      exposure to topiramate (1030 children), 10.5% with exposure to valproate (800
      children), and 4.1% with exposure to lamotrigine (4205 children).
    explanation: >-
      The strongest available control for confounding by indication: topiramate and
      lamotrigine act as internal comparators within mothers with epilepsy, and only
      valproate separates from no exposure.
  - reference: PMID:37666366
    reference_title: "Neurodevelopmental outcomes in children and adults with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder: A contribution from the ConcePTION project."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Rates of neurodevelopmental disorders, particularly autistic spectrum disorders
      (62.9%) and sensory problems (80.6%) are particularly central to the FVSD
      phenotype.
    explanation: >-
      The 62.9% figure quoted in this phenotype's description, which was previously
      asserted without its source attached. The denominator is individuals already
      diagnosed, not exposed pregnancies.
- name: Attention deficit hyperactivity disorder
  category: Behavioral
  frequency: OCCASIONAL
  description: >-
    ADHD at an absolute 15-year risk of 11% after exposure against 4.6%
    unexposed. The relative increase is modest beside the autism signal, and the
    Danish cohort found it for valproate and not for other antiepileptic drugs.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Attention deficit hyperactivity disorder
    term:
      id: HP:0007018
      label: Attention deficit hyperactivity disorder
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:30646190
    reference_title: Association of Prenatal Exposure to Valproate and Other Antiepileptic Drugs With Risk for Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder in Offspring.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The absolute 15-year risk of ADHD was 4.6% (95% CI, 4.5%-4.6%) in children
      unexposed to valproate and 11.0% (95% CI, 8.2%-14.2%) in children who were
      exposed to valproate in pregnancy.
    explanation: >-
      The absolute risks, which is what the frequency band is taken from: 11% sits in
      the OCCASIONAL range.
  - reference: PMID:30646190
    reference_title: Association of Prenatal Exposure to Valproate and Other Antiepileptic Drugs With Risk for Attention-Deficit/Hyperactivity Disorder in Offspring.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "No associations were found between other AEDs and ADHD."
    explanation: >-
      Specificity to valproate among antiepileptic drugs, which argues against the
      maternal illness explaining it.
- name: Motor delay
  category: Neurologic
  frequency: FREQUENT
  description: >-
    Gross motor delay in about 60% and fine motor delay in about 40% of a clinic
    series. Motor difficulty is one of the three domains the consensus says
    warrants formal neuropsychological assessment.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Motor delay
    term:
      id: HP:0001270
      label: Motor delay
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:10882750
    reference_title: A clinical study of 57 children with fetal anticonvulsant syndromes.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Forty four (77%) had learning difficulties, 46 (81%) had speech delay, 34
      (60%) had gross motor delay, and 24 (42%) had fine motor delay.
    explanation: The gross and fine motor frequencies in the same series.
  notes: >-
    The frequency comes from PMID:10882750, a series of 57 children of whom 52
    were recruited through a support-group association and only 34 (60%) had
    valproate monotherapy, the rest carbamazepine, phenytoin or polytherapy. It is
    therefore an upper bound for a fetal anticonvulsant population, not a
    population frequency for valproate.

  category: Neurologic

  - reference: PMID:37666366

    supports: SUPPORT

    explanation: >-

- name: Memory impairment
  category: Neurologic
  description: >-
    Working memory and processing speed are impaired more than a standard
    deviation below expectation in individuals with the diagnosis, and memory was
    one of the domains on which exposed children did worse in the prospective
    NEAD cohort. No source gives a proportion, so no frequency is recorded.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Memory impairment
    term:

      label: Memory impairment

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The mean differences for verbal comprehension (21.07, 95% CI -25.84 to

      (16.87, 95% CI -22.24 to -11.50) performances were poorer than expected with
      the mean differences over one standard deviation from the comparison group.
    explanation: The domain-level deficits including working memory.
  - reference: PMID:23352199

    supports: SUPPORT

  notes: >-
    No frequency is recorded for the facial features. The gestalt contributes to
    the diagnostic criteria, so any frequency measured in diagnosed cohorts is
    partly circular, and the consensus states the features are not required for
    the diagnosis — particularly after low-dose exposure or exposure later than
    facial development. The same reasoning applies to the other facial
    phenotypes in this entry.

- name: Prominent metopic ridge

    that it be looked for because pronounced ridging warrants craniofacial

    supports: SUPPORT

- name: Trigonocephaly
  category: Craniofacial
  description: >-
    Triangular forehead from metopic suture fusion. In a series of 2,220 children
    with craniosynostosis, detailed maternal information was available for 1,676, of
    whom 17 mothers had taken valproate monotherapy — and all 17 children had
    trigonocephaly — an unusually clean association,
    though the series is ascertained on craniosynostosis and so cannot give a
    frequency among exposed children.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Trigonocephaly
    term:
      id: HP:0000243
      label: Trigonocephaly
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:11702867
    reference_title: Craniosynostosis and fetal exposure to sodium valproate.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: "All 17 children exhibited trigonocephaly."
    explanation: >-
      Every valproate-exposed child in a large craniosynostosis series had this
      specific form.
  - reference: PMID:11702867
    reference_title: Craniosynostosis and fetal exposure to sodium valproate.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      In children born with fetal valproate syndrome, it is important to be aware of
      the possibility of metopic suture synostosis, which we believe should be
      considered part of the syndrome, because early surgical intervention may
      improve cognitive outcome.
    explanation: >-
      The authors' argument for counting this as part of the syndrome, with the
      clinical reason it matters.
- name: Craniosynostosis
  category: Craniofacial
  frequency: VERY_RARE
  description: >-
    Premature suture fusion, metopic in the reported cases, at roughly seven
    times the background odds after first-trimester monotherapy.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Craniosynostosis
    term:
      id: HP:0001363
      label: Craniosynostosis
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:20558369
    reference_title: Valproic acid monotherapy in pregnancy and major congenital malformations.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      use of valproic acid monotherapy was associated with significantly increased
      risks for 6 of the 14 malformations under consideration; the adjusted odds
      ratios were as follows: spina bifida, 12.7 (95% confidence interval [CI], 7.7
      to 20.7); atrial septal defect, 2.5 (95% CI, 1.4 to 4.4); cleft palate, 5.2
      (95% CI, 2.8 to 9.9); hypospadias, 4.8 (95% CI, 2.9 to 8.1); polydactyly, 2.2
      (95% CI, 1.0 to 4.5); and craniosynostosis, 6.8 (95% CI, 1.8 to 18.8).
    explanation: >-
      The EUROCAT case-control odds ratios. This one sentence is the source for the
      six malformation phenotypes that follow, which is why it is quoted in each.
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      The risk of cardiac malformations is increased 2–3 fold, that for cleft palate
      increased five-fold, that for craniosynostosis six-fold, and that for
      hypospadias and genitourinary (GU) malformations five-fold.
    explanation: The consensus statement's independent summary of the same risk pattern.
- name: Spina bifida
  category: Neurologic
  frequency: VERY_RARE
  diagnostic: true
  description: >-
    The malformation that first identified valproate as a teratogen and still its
    largest single signal: about thirteen times the background odds after
    first-trimester monotherapy. It is rare in absolute terms — 3% of a
    literature series of affected children — which is the gap between a striking
    relative risk and an uncommon event.

    preferred_term: Spina bifida

- name: Cleft palate
  category: Craniofacial
  frequency: VERY_RARE
  description: >-
    Cleft palate at about five times background odds, and specifically isolated
    cleft palate rather than cleft lip and palate — an eleven-fold increase for
    the isolated form against three-and-a-half-fold for cleft lip and palate. The
    distinction matters mechanistically as well as clinically, since the two have
    different embryology.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Cleft palate
    term:
      id: HP:0000175
      label: Cleft palate

    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Thirteen cases of OFC occurred in 1282 VPA monotherapy-exposed pregnancies in
      the UKEPR; nine had ICP and four had CLP, representing an 11.3-fold and
      3.5-fold increase risk in ICP and CLP, respectively, over general population
      risk.
    explanation: >-
      Separates the two cleft types with their own risk estimates, from a monotherapy
      register cohort.

- name: Joint hypermobility
