- name: Small for gestational age
  category: Growth
  description: >-
    Not established, and curated to record that. A Nordic cohort of 4.5 million
    children found no association between prenatal valproate and either
    small-for-gestational-age birth or microcephaly, and the consensus statement
    separately reports no significant increase in intrauterine growth
    restriction. The AAN practice guideline nevertheless advises avoiding
    valproate partly to reduce small-for-gestational-age risk, so the sources
    disagree. No frequency is recorded: the phenotype may not belong to the
    condition at all, and any band would assert more than the evidence supports.
  phenotype_term:
    preferred_term: Small for gestational age
    term:
      id: HP:0001518
      label: Small for gestational age
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38476755
    reference_title: "Prenatal exposure to antiseizure medications and fetal growth: a population-based cohort study from the Nordic countries."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      No associations with small for gestational age and microcephaly were
      identified after prenatal exposure to lamotrigine, valproate, gabapentin,
      levetiracetam, phenobarbital, acetazolamide, phenytoin, clobazam, primidone,
      zonisamide, vigabatrin, ethosuximide and lacosamide
    explanation: >-
      The largest cohort addressing the question finds no association for valproate,
      which is the primary evidence against this phenotype.
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      The incidence of intrauterine growth retardation and Caesarean section is not
      significantly increased in mothers taking VPA in pregnancy
    explanation: An independent negative statement from the consensus group.
  - reference: PMID:38748979
    reference_title: "Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Clinicians should avoid the use of valproic acid or topiramate in PWECP to
      minimize the risk of offspring being born small for gestational age, if
      clinically feasible.
    explanation: >-
      The guideline recommendation that presupposes the association, carried as the
      opposing evidence rather than omitted.
  notes: >-
    See the fvs_growth_disagreement discussion. Note that the guideline's
    systematic review closed in August 2022 and the Nordic cohort published in
    2024, so the disagreement may be a vintage difference rather than a genuine
    conflict.
genetic:
- name: MTHFR
  gene_term:
    preferred_term: MTHFR
    term:
      id: hgnc:7436
      label: MTHFR
  association: >-
    Maternal 677C>T genotype as a susceptibility modifier; the child's genotype
    shows no effect
  relationship_type: SUSCEPTIBILITY
  notes: >-
    The unusual feature of this association is whose genome it is in: it is a
    maternal genotype effect, not inherited susceptibility in the affected child. Two
    independent studies found the risk attached to the mother's MTHFR genotype
    and not the child's, which is what a maternal one-carbon metabolism effect on
    the intrauterine environment should look like rather than a susceptibility
    allele in the affected individual. It is recorded as a modifier of a
    teratogenic exposure, never as a cause: the same study that found the
    maternal effect put the valproate odds ratio at 7.79 against 2.57 for the
    genotype, and concluded the main teratogenic effects run through other
    mechanisms. A knock-in mouse supports the interaction directly. Nothing here
    supports genotyping mothers clinically.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:17853476
    reference_title: "Fetal anticonvulsant syndromes and polymorphisms in MTHFR, MTR, and MTRR."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The risk of having a child with congenital malformation or FACS was three to
      four times higher for mothers who were MTHFR 677TT homozygotes compared with
      MTHFR 677CC homozygotes.
    explanation: The maternal genotype effect, in case-parent triads.
  - reference: PMID:17853476
    reference_title: "Fetal anticonvulsant syndromes and polymorphisms in MTHFR, MTR, and MTRR."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      No effect of the child's genotype on congenital malformation,
      neurodevelopmental disorder or FACS was detected using this method.
    explanation: >-
      The negative result for the child's genotype, which is why this is curated as
      a maternal effect rather than as susceptibility in the affected individual.
  - reference: PMID:17951123
    reference_title: Influence of the MTHFR genotype on the rate of malformations following exposure to antiepileptic drugs in utero.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      This study indicates that although the maternal MTHFR genotype may confer
      susceptibility to the teratogenic effect of AEDs, particularly VPA, it is
      likely that the main teratogenic effects are mediated through other
      mechanisms.
    explanation: >-
      An independent replication with an explicit statement bounding how much the
      genotype explains.
  - reference: PMID:17951123
    reference_title: Influence of the MTHFR genotype on the rate of malformations following exposure to antiepileptic drugs in utero.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      There was no association between the child's MTHFR genotype and the rate of
      MM.
    explanation: The same negative finding for the child's genotype in a second cohort.
  - reference: PMID:40645457
    reference_title: "Influence of methylenetetrahydrofolate 677C > T variant in murine models following valproic acid exposure."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Therefore, intraperitoneal injected VPA significantly increased the
      malformation rate of CT and TT heterozygote and homozygote mice,
      respectively, compared to that of CC mice (P < 0.05).
    explanation: >-
      A genotype-by-exposure interaction in an engineered mouse, which is the
      experimental counterpart of the human association.
diagnosis:
- name: Documented valproate exposure during pregnancy with exclusion of alternatives
  description: >-
    The diagnosis is clinical and has no confirmatory test. It requires
    documented exposure, a compatible pattern, and the exclusion of other
    explanations — in practice a normal chromosomal microarray and Fragile X
    study. The exclusion step is not a formality: in one audit of 40 exposed
    individuals, six had a different genetic cause of their developmental delay,
    and the majority did not meet the threshold for the diagnosis at all.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      The diagnosis of FVSD is difficult, as there is no specific biomarker that can
      be assayed in this condition to prove diagnosis, though there are conditions
      with overlapping features which need to be excluded
    explanation: States both the absence of a test and the need for exclusion.
  - reference: PMID:35863137
    reference_title: Establishing or Excluding a Diagnosis of Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder is a Multi-layered Process.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Six of the forty patients (15%) had an alternative genetic cause of
      developmental delay established.
    explanation: >-
      Quantifies how often exposure plus delay turns out to have another cause,
      which is the reason exclusion is required rather than advisable.
  - reference: PMID:35863137
    reference_title: Establishing or Excluding a Diagnosis of Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder is a Multi-layered Process.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      An equal awareness is warranted that developmental delay in the context of VPA
      exposure in pregnancy does not necessarily constitute a diagnosis of FVSD but
      that other competing diagnostic hypotheses have to be considered.
    explanation: The authors' statement of the inferential error this criterion guards against.
- name: Facial gestalt recognition
  description: >-
    The facial pattern makes the diagnosis more secure but is explicitly not
    required for it, particularly after a low dose or after exposure beginning
    later than facial development. This is the clinical counterpart of the
    entry's ordering: the neurodevelopmental arm is the larger one and does not
    depend on the face.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Due to these points the expert consensus group felt that whilst the presence
      of a typical facial presentation makes the diagnosis more certain, typical
      facial features are not absolutely required to be able to diagnose that an
      individual has been affected by exposure to VPA in utero, particularly if the
      dose of exposure was low or if the exposure occurred after the period of fetal
      facial development.
    explanation: The consensus position on how much diagnostic weight the face carries.
- name: Peripheral blood DNA methylation episignature
  description: >-
    A classifier trained on blood methylation distinguishes exposed affected
    individuals from controls and from a reference set of other syndromes with
    episignatures. It is the first objective candidate biomarker for a condition
    that has none, and it is a research tool rather than standard care. Its
    enrichment for cadherin-superfamily cell-adhesion genes is suggestive beside
    the cell-junction mechanism curated in the pathophysiology, but neither
    source connects them.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39097820
    reference_title: Discovery of DNA methylation signature in the peripheral blood of individuals with history of antenatal exposure to valproic acid.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Genomic DNA methylation analysis was applied, and a distinct DNA methylation
      profile was identified in the majority of affected individuals.
    explanation: >-
      The signature and its coverage — the majority, not all, which bounds its use
      as a test.
  - reference: PMID:39097820
    reference_title: Discovery of DNA methylation signature in the peripheral blood of individuals with history of antenatal exposure to valproic acid.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      The valproate exposure episignature exhibited high sensitivity and specificity
      relative to a large reference data set of unaffected controls and individuals
      with a wide spectrum of syndromic disorders with episignatures.
    explanation: >-
      The discrimination that matters clinically is against other syndromes, not
      only against healthy controls.
  - reference: PMID:39097820
    reference_title: Discovery of DNA methylation signature in the peripheral blood of individuals with history of antenatal exposure to valproic acid.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Gene set enrichment analysis demonstrated an enrichment for terms associated
      with cell adhesion, including significant overrepresentation of the cadherin
      superfamily.
    explanation: >-
      The enriched gene set, recorded because it names the same biology as the
      cell-junction node without the paper making that link.
- name: Targeted fetal anomaly ultrasound and structured postnatal examination
  description: >-
    Prenatally, detailed scanning with the sonographer told about the exposure.
    Postnatally, a directed examination for the specific anomalies rather than a
    general newborn check — direct visualization of the palate including
    submucous cleft, radial ray, talipes and metopic ridging.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Special care should be taken to visualise the palate directly and to check for
      submucous cleft palate, to look for radial ray defects, talipes and ridging of
      the metopic suture indicating metopic synostosis.
    explanation: The specific postnatal examination the consensus recommends.
differential_diagnoses:
- name: Fetal alcohol spectrum disorder
  disease_term:
    preferred_term: fetal alcohol spectrum disorder
    term:
      id: MONDO:0000408
      label: fetal alcohol spectrum disorder
  description: >-
    The other common teratogenic cause of facial dysmorphism with
    neurodevelopmental impairment. The lip and philtrum features overlap
    directly, and both conditions depend on exposure history rather than on a
    test, so a candid exposure history is what separates them.
  distinguishing_features:
  - >-
      Short palpebral fissures and the smooth philtrum plus thin upper lip triad
      characterize fetal alcohol spectrum disorder; the valproate gestalt adds an
      everted lower lip, metopic ridging and arched eyebrows, and carries a
      specific malformation set — spina bifida, isolated cleft palate, radial ray
      defects — that fetal alcohol spectrum disorder does not.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      The facial dysmorphism is characterised by a broad nasal bridge, short nose
      with forward-facing (anteverted) nostrils, small mouth with thin upper lip,
      everted lower lip flat philtrum, ridging of the metopic suture and neatly
      arched eyebrows
    explanation: >-
      The valproate gestalt against which the alcohol one is compared. The
      comparison itself is this entry's, not the source's.
- name: Chromatinopathies
  description: >-
    Mendelian disorders of the epigenetic machinery, Kleefstra syndrome among
    them. The overlap is not coincidental: valproate inhibits histone
    deacetylases, and these disorders disrupt the same chromatin regulation
    genetically, so the phenotypic resemblance may reflect a shared perturbed
    mechanism. This is the differential that makes the mandatory genetic testing
    worth doing rather than defensive.
  distinguishing_features:
  - >-
      A causative variant on sequencing, and absence of documented valproate
      exposure. The valproate episignature is a candidate discriminator since
      several chromatinopathies have their own episignatures.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:33959609
    reference_title: Chromatin Imbalance as the Vertex Between Fetal Valproate Syndrome and Chromatinopathies.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Critically examining the phenotype of FVSD and chromatinopathies, they shared
      several overlapping features that can be observed despite the different
      etiologies of these disorders, suggesting the possible existence of a common
      perturbed mechanism(s) during embryonic development.
    explanation: >-
      States both the phenotypic overlap and the mechanistic reason for it, which is
      why this differential is curated with the mechanism rather than only as a
      look-alike.
- name: Fetal carbamazepine syndrome
  disease_term:
    preferred_term: fetal carbamazepine syndrome
    term:
      id: MONDO:0018263
      label: fetal carbamazepine syndrome
  description: >-
    The other antiseizure medication embryofetopathy. Both belong to the older
    category of fetal anticonvulsant syndrome, and the clinic series that
    describe the phenotype often mix them.
  distinguishing_features:
  - >-
      Carbamazepine carries about half the malformation rate of valproate (5.4%
      against 9.9% in EURAP) and, unlike valproate, is not associated with reduced
      IQ or with autism in the prospective cohorts. Exposure history separates them.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38497990
    reference_title: Risk of Major Congenital Malformations and Exposure to Antiseizure Medication Monotherapy.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      MCMs occurred in 153 of 1549 pregnancies for valproate (9.9%; 95% CI,
      8.5%-11.5%), 9 of 142 for phenytoin (6.3%; 95% CI, 3.4%-11.6%), 21 of 338 for
      phenobarbital (6.2%; 95% CI, 4.1%-9.3%), 121 of 2255 for carbamazepine (5.4%;
      95% CI, 4.5%-6.4%), 10 of 204 for topiramate (4.9%; 95% CI, 2.7%-8.8%), 110 of
      3584 for lamotrigine (3.1%; 95% CI, 2.5%-3.7%), 13 of 443 for oxcarbazepine
      (2.9%; 95% CI, 1.7%-5.0%), and 33 of 1325 for levetiracetam (2.5%; 95% CI,
      1.8%-3.5%).
    explanation: >-
      The malformation rates by drug, which is the quantitative basis for separating
      valproate from the other antiseizure medication embryopathies.
  - reference: PMID:23352199
    reference_title: "Fetal antiepileptic drug exposure and cognitive outcomes at age 6 years (NEAD study): a prospective observational study."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      age-6 IQ was lower after exposure to valproate (mean 97, 95% CI 94-101) than
      to carbamazepine
    explanation: The cognitive separation between the two exposures in a prospective cohort.
treatments:
- name: Avoidance of valproate before conception
  description: >-
    The only intervention that prevents the condition, and the reason the entry
    records a HIGH burden for something entirely avoidable. Guidance is
    directive: avoid valproate in people of childbearing potential where
    clinically feasible, and use lamotrigine, levetiracetam or oxcarbazepine
    instead. Timing is the whole of it. In Australian register data,
    malformation rates were 4.5% where valproate was reduced or withdrawn before
    pregnancy against 10.9% where it was unchanged, and 2.7% in the subgroup where it
    was stopped altogether. The cost falls on seizure control: in that same changed
    group — the 4.5% one, not the 2.7% subgroup — seizure-affected pregnancies rose
    from 31% to 46%. This is a trade, not a free improvement. Once a
    pregnancy has started the calculus reverses, and the guideline cautions
    against removing an effective drug.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Preventive Intervention
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15843
      label: Preventive Intervention
  therapeutic_modality: OTHER
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38748979
    reference_title: "Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Clinicians must avoid the use of valproic acid in PWECP to minimize the risk
      of MCMs or neural tube defects (NTDs), if clinically feasible.
    explanation: The guideline's strongest-level recommendation on the malformation arm.
  - reference: PMID:38748979
    reference_title: "Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Clinicians must consider using lamotrigine, levetiracetam, or oxcarbazepine in
      PWECP when appropriate based on the patient's epilepsy syndrome, likelihood of
      achieving seizure control, and comorbidities, to minimize the risk of MCMs.
    explanation: Names the alternatives, which is what makes avoidance actionable.
  - reference: PMID:32314363
    reference_title: Pregnancy after valproate withdrawal-Fetal malformations and seizure control.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Although the available data have limitations, fetal malformation rates in the
      pregnancies studied were lower in the VPA changed pregnancies (4.5%) than in
      the VPA unchanged comparator pregnancies (10.9%, hazard ratio [HR] 0.412, 95%
      confidence interval [CI] 0.190-0.892), and were only 2.7% where VPA intake was
      ceased before pregnancy
    explanation: Quantifies the benefit of stopping before conception.
  - reference: PMID:32314363
    reference_title: Pregnancy after valproate withdrawal-Fetal malformations and seizure control.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Seizure-affected pregnancies were more frequent in the VPA changed pregnancies
      than in the VPA unchanged ones (46.2% vs 30.8%, HR 1.500, 95% CI 1.203-1.870).
    explanation: >-
      The cost side of the same decision, carried so this treatment is not recorded
      as benefit without harm.
  - reference: PMID:38748979
    reference_title: "Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Once a PWECP is already pregnant, clinicians should exercise caution in
      attempting to remove or replace an ASM that is effective in controlling
      generalized tonic-clonic or focal-to-bilateral tonic-clonic seizures.
    explanation: >-
      The reversal of the recommendation after conception, which is why this
      treatment is specifically preconceptional.
  notes: >-
    Lowest effective dose where valproate cannot be avoided, since risk is
    dose-dependent across the whole range with no risk-free band.

    NCIT:C15843 (Preventive Intervention) is true of this action but does not
    capture that the action is an omission — not prescribing a drug rather than
    administering one. No NCIT clinical-action term expresses that, so the omission
    is stated in the description instead.
- name: Periconceptional folic acid supplementation
  description: >-
    Recommended universally for people on antiseizure medication, with the
    consensus specifying 0.4-5 mg daily from two to three months before
    conception until twelve weeks. The recommendation is firmer than the evidence
    behind it for this particular exposure: high-dose folic acid before pregnancy
    reduced malformations across antiseizure medications generally, while the
    consensus calls the evidence for preventing valproate teratogenesis
    conflicting and a case-control study found no protection against
    valproate-associated spina bifida.
  action_category: THERAPEUTIC
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Preventative Nutritional Supplementation
    term:
      id: NCIT:C64894
      label: Preventative Nutritional Supplementation
    therapeutic_agent:
    - preferred_term: folic acid
      term:
        id: CHEBI:27470
        label: folic acid
  therapeutic_modality: SMALL_MOLECULE
  target_mechanisms:
  - target: Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism
  notes: >-
    NCIT:C64894 names the preventive supplementation but not the dose schedule or
    the window, which are in the description. The agent is bound separately in
    therapeutic_agent.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38748979
    reference_title: "Teratogenesis, Perinatal, and Neurodevelopmental Outcomes After In Utero Exposure to Antiseizure Medication: Practice Guideline From the AAN, AES, and SMFM."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      Clinicians should prescribe at least 0.4 mg of folic acid supplementation
      daily preconceptionally and during pregnancy to any PWECP treated with an ASM
    explanation: The guideline recommendation and its minimum dose.
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: >-
      It is recommended that folic acid, taken in doses 0.4-5 mg daily should be
      taken from 2 to 3 months before conception and should be continued until 12
      weeks of pregnancy.
    explanation: The dose range and the window.
  - reference: PMID:31324220
    reference_title: "Diagnosis and management of individuals with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder; a consensus statement from the European Reference Network for Congenital Malformations and Intellectual Disability."
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: OTHER
    snippet: "The evidence of high dose folic acid in preventing VPA teratogenesis is conflicting"
    explanation: >-
      The same consensus statement's verdict on the evidence, carried so the
      recommendation is not read as settled efficacy.
- name: Surgical correction of structural malformations
  description: >-
    Repair of the malformations as they arise: neural tube defect closure, cleft
    palate repair, hypospadias repair, cardiac repair, and craniofacial surgery
    for metopic synostosis. The craniosynostosis case is the one where timing has
    been argued to matter beyond the structural result — in the valproate-exposed
    trigonocephaly series, IQ was higher in those operated before six months,
    which is an observational association in a small series rather than a
    demonstrated cognitive benefit.

    explanation: >-

- name: Multidisciplinary developmental and medical surveillance
  description: >-
    Not a treatment so much as the organizing principle of care: scheduled review
    of growth, development, hearing and vision, neuropsychological assessment
    across cognition, language, memory, attention and executive function, and
    autism screening at around eighteen months. The consensus exists because this
    coordination was absent, and it says plainly that the management literature
    is lacking — so this is expert opinion, not trial evidence.
  action_category: MONITORING

    explanation: The content of the neuropsychological surveillance by age.

- name: Genetic counselling and recurrence risk discussion
  description: >-
    Two distinct conversations. For the affected individual there is no
    heritable risk to pass on. For the mother, a previous affected child raises
    the risk in a subsequent pregnancy — 16.8% after one malformed child and 50%
    after two in the UK register, with a non-significant trend to higher
    recurrence specifically for valproate.
  action_category: COUNSELING_INFORMATIONAL
  treatment_term:
    preferred_term: Genetic Counseling
    term:
      id: NCIT:C15240
      label: Genetic Counseling
  therapeutic_modality: OTHER

    supports: SUPPORT

      For women whose first child had a congenital malformation there was a 16.8%
      risk of having another child with a congenital malformation, compared with
      9.8% for women whose first child did not have a malformation (relative risk
      1.73, 95% confidence interval [CI] 1.01-2.96).
    explanation: The recurrence figure that the counselling conveys.
  - reference: PMID:23167802
    reference_title: Recurrence risk of congenital malformations in infants exposed to antiepileptic drugs in utero.
    supports: SUPPORT

    snippet: >-

      pregnancies exposed to valproate (21.9%, relative risk 1.47, 95% CI 0.68-3.20)
    explanation: >-
      The valproate-specific recurrence, with an interval crossing one, which is why
      the general figure is the one counselled.
animal_models:
- name: Prenatal valproate rat model of autism
  species: Rattus norvegicus
  genotype: Wild type
  background: Wistar and Sprague-Dawley
  category: Teratogen exposure model
  publication: PMID:15238991
  description: >-
    A single dose around embryonic day 12.5 produces reduced social behaviour,
    stereotypy, altered sensory thresholds and delayed maturation. This is the
    most-used environmental model of autism in neuroscience, which cuts both
    ways here: the behavioural validation is unusually thorough, and almost all
    of it was done to model idiopathic autism rather than this syndrome.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Neurodevelopmental Impairment
    relationship: PARTIALLY_RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: ORGANISM
    description: >-
      Reproduces the behavioural end of the neurodevelopmental arm, including the
      sensory abnormalities that the human cohort data put at 80% of diagnosed
      individuals. Pointed at the organism-scale outcome node rather than at circuit
      formation, because what this model observes is behaviour.
    limitations: >-
      A single high intraperitoneal bolus at one developmental moment is not the
      continuous, variable oral exposure of a human pregnancy, and the model was
      built and validated against idiopathic autism rather than against fetal
      valproate syndrome. It says nothing about the malformation arm, and the
      behaviours scored are rodent analogues of human constructs.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:15238991
      reference_title: "Behavioral alterations in rats prenatally exposed to valproic acid: animal model of autism."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        The results of the present experiments demonstrate that VPA rats exhibit:
        (1) lower sensitivity to pain and higher sensitivity to nonpainful stimuli,
        (2) diminished acoustic prepulse inhibition, (3) locomotor and
        repetitive/stereotypic-like hyperactivity combined with lower exploratory
        activity, and (4) decreased number of social behaviors and increased latency
        to social behaviors.
      explanation: The behavioural battery that defines what this model recapitulates.
  - target: Disordered Cortical Neurogenesis
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: TISSUE
    description: >-
      The same exposure and timing reduce Purkinje cell number in the cerebellar
      vermis, with the posterior lobe affected more than the anterior — a
      regional gradient rather than a global loss.
    limitations: >-
      The human comparison is autism neuropathology, not neuropathology from
      individuals with fetal valproate syndrome, so the parallel is to a related
      condition.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:10840175
      reference_title: Prenatal exposure of rats to valproic acid reproduces the cerebellar anomalies associated with autism.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        Within the vermis, the reduction in volume was significantly greater in the
        posterior lobe than in the anterior lobe.
      explanation: The regional specificity, which is what makes this more than a size difference.
- name: Mthfr 677C>T knock-in mouse with valproate exposure
  species: Mus musculus
  genotype: Mthfr677C>T knock-in, CC, CT and TT
  category: Gene-environment interaction model
  publication: PMID:40645457
  genes:
  - preferred_term: MTHFR
    term:
      id: hgnc:7436
      label: MTHFR
  description: >-
    The experimental counterpart of the human maternal-genotype association: mice
    carrying the common MTHFR variant malform more after the same valproate dose
    than wild-type littermates, and show the expected biochemistry — lower
    enzyme activity and raised homocysteine.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Disrupted Embryonic One-Carbon Metabolism
    relationship: PERTURBS
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: ORGANISM
    description: >-
      Tests the folate branch by varying one-carbon capacity genetically instead of
      varying the drug, which is the more informative direction.
    limitations: >-
      A single 400 mg/kg intraperitoneal dose at E7.5, and the readout is
      malformation rate rather than a measurement of folate status in the embryo.
      The human association is with the mother's genotype; in the mouse, dam and
      fetus share it, so the experiment cannot separate maternal from fetal
      contribution.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:40645457
      reference_title: "Influence of methylenetetrahydrofolate 677C > T variant in murine models following valproic acid exposure."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        Consistent with the clinical data, Mthfr677C>T mice also showed lower liver
        MTHFR enzymatic activity and elevated serum homocysteine (Hcy) levels.
      explanation: Confirms the engineered allele does what it should before the exposure is applied.
- name: Mouse neural tube defect model with NRF2 induction
  species: Mus musculus
  genotype: Wild type
  background: CD-1 and C57BL/6J
  category: Teratogen exposure model with pharmacological rescue
  publication: PMID:34737154
  description: >-
    Valproate exposure produces open neural tubes and a transiently oxidising
    glutathione redox potential; pretreatment with an NRF2 inducer prevents both.
    The rescue is what makes the redox branch a candidate mechanism rather than a
    correlate.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Embryonic Redox Disruption
    relationship: RESCUES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    description: >-
      Intervening on the antioxidant response, not on the drug, reduces both the
      redox shift and the defect.
    limitations: >-
      The mouse defect is exencephaly, a cranial failure, while the characteristic
      human lesion is lumbosacral spina bifida, so the axial level differs. D3T
      induces a broad transcriptional programme, so the rescue is not specific to
      glutathione redox state. The two halves of the result also come from different
      preparations: the redox measurement from CD-1 whole-embryo culture with
      valproate applied in vitro, and the defect rescue from C57BL/6J dams dosed in
      utero, so no single experiment shows both in one strain and system.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:34737154
      reference_title: NRF2 activation inhibits valproic acid-induced neural tube defects in mice.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        These data show that NRF2 induction via D3T pretreatment protects against
        VPA-induced redox dysregulation and decreases the prevalence of NTDs in
        developing mouse embryos.
      explanation: The authors' statement of the rescue in both the mechanism and the outcome.
- name: Rat cranial neural crest exposure model
  species: Rattus norvegicus
  genotype: Wild type
  category: Stage-specific teratogen exposure model
  publication: PMID:38403785
  description: >-
    A single dose immediately before cranial crest formation, read out by
    whole-mount in situ hybridization and immunohistochemistry for crest
    markers. The design is what distinguishes it: dosing is timed to the
    developmental event rather than to a convenient day, so the result is
    stage-specific.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Disturbed Cranial Neural Crest Formation and Migration
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    description: >-
      Shows both halves of the node — reduced crest formation at midbrain-rhombomere
      1/2 and misrouted rhombomere 4 crest carrying the wrong positional identity.
    limitations: >-
      Embryonic readouts over hours to days, with no follow-through to a formed
      face, so the connection to the human gestalt is inferred. Rat and human
      craniofacial development differ in the relative contribution of the
      frontonasal mass.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:38403785
      reference_title: Effect of valproic acid on the formation and migration of cranial neural crest cells at the early developmental stages in rat embryos.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
      snippet: >-
        In vitro verification experiments using the whole embryo culture system
        revealed that midbrain-R4 NCC migration was abnormal.
      explanation: An independent verification of the migration abnormality in cultured embryos.
experimental_models:
- name: Genotyped human neural progenitor population exposed to valproic acid
  experimental_model_type: PRIMARY_CELL_CULTURE
  organism:
    preferred_term: human
    term:
      id: NCBITaxon:9606
      label: Homo sapiens
  cell_source: Primary human neural progenitor cells from 83 genotyped multi-ancestry donors
  culture_system: Monolayer, 48-hour exposure to 1 mM valproic acid
  publication: PMID:41935183
  description: >-
    A population rather than a cell line, which is the point: with 83 genotyped
    donors the design can separate what the drug does in everyone from what it
    does only in some genetic backgrounds. Valproate remodelled two-thirds of
    measured chromatin peaks, reduced proliferation in every donor at a clinically
    relevant concentration, and shifted cells toward differentiation. Genetic
    variation modified the response at over a thousand loci, and a
    transcriptome-wide association in the valproate condition implicated folate
    metabolism genes in cognitive ability — which lands the two mechanistic
    branches curated here, chromatin and folate, in one human experiment.
  cell_types:
  - preferred_term: neural progenitor cell
    term:
      id: CL:0011020
      label: neural progenitor cell
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Embryonic Histone Hyperacetylation and Ectopic Transcription
    relationship: MEASURES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: MOLECULAR
    description: >-
      Measures the chromatin response directly in human cells, and finds it is not
      the simple opening that HDAC inhibition alone would predict.
    limitations: >-
      A single cell type and a 48-hour acute exposure, which the authors state does
      not model chronic clinical administration. Chromatin accessibility is not
      histone acetylation, so the mark itself is inferred rather than measured.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:41935183
      reference_title: Assessing molecular gene by treatment interactions using a population of neural progenitors exposed to valproic acid and lithium.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        Our use of hNPCs, while highly relevant for studying the teratogenic effects
        of prenatal VPA exposure and the proliferative impacts of Li, represents only
        a single cell type [5, 21].
      explanation: The authors' own statement of the first limitation.
    - reference: PMID:41935183
      reference_title: Assessing molecular gene by treatment interactions using a population of neural progenitors exposed to valproic acid and lithium.
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        Furthermore, our 48-hour exposure captures acute molecular responses but may
        not reflect chronic effects of long-term clinical administration [5, 18].
      explanation: The authors' own statement of the exposure-duration limitation.
  - target: Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    description: >-
      The proliferation reduction is present in every donor at 1 mM, so it is a
      property of the exposure rather than of a susceptible subset.
    limitations: >-
      Proliferation in dissociated culture is not neurogenesis in a developing
      cortex, and the direction conflicts with what mouse cortex shows in vivo.
    readouts:
    - name: Proportion of cells in S phase by EdU incorporation
      target: Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
      direction: DECREASED
      interpretation: >-
        Consistent across all donors at a clinically relevant concentration, with
        near-complete arrest at the highest concentrations.
      evidence:
      - reference: PMID:41935183

        supports: SUPPORT

        snippet: >-
          VPA exposure leads to consistent reductions in proliferation across all
          donors at clinically relevant concentrations
        explanation: The measured readout and its consistency across the donor panel.

    explanation: The design, which is what distinguishes this from a single-line exposure study.

    explanation: >-
      Connects the exposure's molecular effect to cognitive ability through the
      folate pathway, which is the branch the clinical prevention advice targets.
- name: Human spinal cord organoid model of valproate-induced neurulation failure
  experimental_model_type: ORGANOID
  organism:

      id: NCBITaxon:9606
      label: Homo sapiens
  cell_source: Human pluripotent stem cell-derived spinal cord organoids
  publication: PMID:37643760
  description: >-
    Organoids that recapitulate aspects of human neurulation and fail to do so
    under valproate, with cell-cell junction genes and proteins the main thing
    that changes. The result is cross-validated in mouse embryos showing the same
    gene changes and the same defect, which is what makes it more than an
    in-vitro curiosity.
  modeled_mechanisms:

    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR

      Identifies the junctional programme as the thing that changes, in human
      tissue, during the process that fails.
    limitations: >-

- name: Human single-rosette brain organoid model of apical constriction failure
  experimental_model_type: ORGANOID
  organism:
    preferred_term: human
    term:
      id: NCBITaxon:9606
      label: Homo sapiens
  cell_source: Human pluripotent stem cell-derived self-organizing single-rosette spheroids
  publication: PMID:37443734
  description: >-
    Self-organizing single-rosette spheroids in which valproate enlarges the apical
    lumen by reducing apical constriction, the same cell behaviour affected by loss
    of SHROOM3. The convergence of a teratogen and a known genetic cause of neural
    tube defects on one cell behaviour is what the model contributes. Registered here
    because its findings are cited as evidence on two mechanism nodes and one edge in
    this entry; the Anencephaly entry uses the same system.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity
    relationship: MEASURES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    description: >-
      Measures apical constriction directly, by lumen size, and shows a teratogenic
      and a genetic cause of neural tube defects converging on it.
    limitations: >-
      The spheroids have no dorsoventral or rostrocaudal patterning, so the model
      cannot speak to the axial level at which the human defect occurs — which is the
      central limitation for this disease, whose characteristic lesion is lumbosacral.
      Lumen size is a proxy for a cell behaviour rather than a measurement of
      closure: no neural tube either closes or fails to close in this system.
    evidence:
    - reference: PMID:37443734
      reference_title: "A Shared Pathogenic Mechanism for Valproic Acid and SHROOM3 Knockout in a Brain Organoid Model of Neural Tube Defects."
      supports: SUPPORT
      evidence_source: IN_VITRO
      snippet: >-
        The increased lumen sizes were caused by reduced cell apical constriction
        suggesting that impingement of this process is a shared mechanism for VPA
        treatment and SHROOM3-KO, two well-known causes of NTDs.
      explanation: >-
        The measured readout and the convergence claim, stated with the authors' own
        hedge intact.
- name: Human dorsal forebrain organoid model of outer radial glia depletion
  experimental_model_type: ORGANOID
  organism:
    preferred_term: human
    term:

  publication: PMID:36523605
  description: >-
    Exposure reduces organoid size and progenitor expansion dose-dependently, and
    specifically depletes outer radial glia — the progenitor subtype responsible
    for the expansion of the human neocortex, and one that rodent models
    represent poorly. The transcriptional changes overlap genes dysregulated in

      id: CL:0000681
      label: radial glial cell

    limitations: >-
      Organoids are immature, lack vasculature, microglia and long-range inputs, and

- name: Directly reprogrammed human neurons with staged valproate exposure
  experimental_model_type: IPSC_DERIVED_MODEL
  organism:
    preferred_term: human
    term:
      id: NCBITaxon:9606
      label: Homo sapiens
  cell_source: Human pluripotent stem cell-derived induced neurons at defined maturation stages
  publication: PMID:31155484
  description: >-
    The design answers a question the clinical data pose but cannot settle: why
    exposure after organogenesis still harms neurodevelopment. By exposing human
    neurons at defined maturation stages it shows the damage is restricted to
    immature neurons, and identifies MARCKSL1 as the mediator downstream of HDAC
    and GSK-3 inhibition.
  modeled_mechanisms:
  - target: Impaired Dendritic Morphogenesis and Synapse Maturation
    relationship: RECAPITULATES
    fidelity: MODERATE
    model_scale: CELLULAR
    description: >-
      Establishes the developmental restriction by direct comparison of immature and
      mature neurons in the same system.
    limitations: >-
      Directly reprogrammed neurons mature on an accelerated, artificial schedule
      that may not map onto fetal timing, and single cultured neurons lack the
      circuit context in which dendritic deficits would produce a phenotype.
    readouts:

      target: Impaired Dendritic Morphogenesis and Synapse Maturation
      direction: DECREASED
      interpretation: >-
        Impaired in developing neurons and unaffected in mature ones, which is the
        comparison that makes the mechanism developmentally restricted.

        snippet: >-
          VPA exposure caused chronic impairment of dendritic morphology and
          functional properties of developing neurons, but not those of mature
          neurons.
        explanation: Both arms of the comparison in one sentence.
discussions:
- discussion_id: fvs_mechanism_attribution_across_entries
  kind: CONTROVERSY
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    Three existing dismech entries record maternal valproate exposure and
    describe its mechanism differently. Anencephaly says the responsible step is
    unsettled between folate antagonism and histone deacetylase inhibition.
    Spina_Bifida_Cystica says valproate acts through HDAC inhibition independent
    of maternal folate status. Autism_Spectrum_Disorder links it to a molecular
    node with unknown intermediates. This entry curates three branches in
    parallel — HDAC inhibition, folate antagonism and redox disruption — without
    ranking them. Should the four entries be reconciled, and if so should the
    shared mechanism become a module rather than being restated in each?
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Transplacental Valproate Exposure of the Embryo
  rationale: >-
    The disagreement is partly real and partly an artifact of each entry citing
    the source nearest to its own disease. Spina_Bifida_Cystica's independence
    claim rests on a review sentence calling HDAC inhibition a proposal, and it
    cites no folate study of its own — its sources there are PMID:27189655 and
    PMID:23790957. The case-control study usually offered for folate independence
    (PMID:20680999, cited in this entry but not in that one) explicitly declines to
    conclude that folate has no effect, and a blinded mouse rescue with folinic acid
    (PMID:3110521) points the other way. Meanwhile the strongest discriminating evidence is of a kind none of the
    three entries cites: structure-activity series across valproate derivatives
    in which HDAC inhibitory potency tracks teratogenic potency (PMID:16485903,
    PMID:15901671), and a parallel dissociation on the folate side where a
    non-teratogenic antiepileptic analogue leaves embryonic folate metabolism
    alone (PMID:1574172). Both branches have that form of evidence, which is why
    this entry ranks neither. A mechanism module would let the four entries share
    one account instead of maintaining four.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:16485903
    reference_title: "Teratogenic effects mediated by inhibition of histone deacetylases: evidence from quantitative structure activity relationships of 20 valproic acid derivatives."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      We also show a quantitative correlation between the IC50(HDAC) and the
      teratogenic potential of VPA derivatives, which clearly points toward HDACs as
      the formerly described teratogenic receptors of VPA-induced neural tube
      defects (NTDs).
    explanation: The structure-activity evidence on the HDAC side.
  - reference: PMID:1574172
    reference_title: "Alteration of embryonic folate metabolism by valproic acid during organogenesis: implications for mechanism of teratogenesis."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      A closely related structural analog of VPA (2-en-VPA, 2-propyl-2-pentenoic
      acid), which exhibits antiepileptic activity but not teratogenicity, did not
      influence the embryonic folate metabolism.
    explanation: >-
      The equivalent dissociation on the folate side, which is why neither branch
      can be dismissed on this kind of evidence.
- discussion_id: fvs_progenitor_direction_conflict
  kind: CONTROVERSY
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    Does valproate push neural progenitors toward differentiation or hold them in
    the cycle? Human neural progenitors from 83 donors, human forebrain organoids
    and rat cortical progenitors all show reduced proliferation with premature
    differentiation. Mouse cortex in vivo shows the opposite: progenitors fail to
    exit the cycle on schedule, yielding excess superficial-layer neurons. Which
    describes human fetal cortex?
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Altered Neural Progenitor Proliferation and Differentiation
  rationale: >-
    This is not a minor discrepancy; the two accounts predict opposite
    cytoarchitecture. The species is one candidate explanation, the system
    another — dissociated or organoid culture against intact developing cortex —
    and exposure duration a third, since the human work is acute and the mouse
    work is in utero. The node is written as a disturbance of balance rather than
    a direction because of this, and the conflicting result is carried as a
    REFUTE item on the node rather than omitted. Resolving it would need a
    directional readout in intact human fetal tissue, which does not exist.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:41935183
    reference_title: Assessing molecular gene by treatment interactions using a population of neural progenitors exposed to valproic acid and lithium.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      This finding is consistent with VPA inducing a developmental shift in hNPCs,
      promoting early differentiation while suppressing proliferation.
    explanation: One direction, in human cells.
  - reference: PMID:27798144
    reference_title: In Utero Exposure to Valproic Acid Induces Neocortical Dysgenesis via Dysregulation of Neural Progenitor Cell Proliferation/Differentiation.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      inhibits the cell cycle exit of neural progenitor cells during the early stage
      of neocortical histogenesis
    explanation: The other direction, in intact mouse cortex.
- discussion_id: fvs_axial_level_mismatch
  kind: HUMAN_MODEL_MISMATCH
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    Rodent valproate models produce exencephaly, a cranial neural tube defect,
    while the characteristic human lesion is lumbosacral spina bifida. Does the
    difference in axial level limit what the rodent neural tube work establishes
    about the human defect?
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Failed Neural Tube Closure
  rationale: >-
    Cranial and spinal neuropore closure use overlapping but distinct cellular
    mechanisms and close at different times, so a mechanism demonstrated for
    mouse exencephaly does not automatically transfer to human spina bifida. Most
    of the mechanistic evidence feeding this node — the folinic acid rescue, the
    NRF2 rescue, the folate metabolite work — is from models scoring exencephaly.
    The tension is already live inside dismech: Anencephaly.yaml records maternal
    valproate as TRIGGERS on failure of cranial neuropore closure in humans, so the
    repository asserts both a cranial and a lumbosacral human outcome for the same
    exposure, and the rodent evidence maps onto the cranial one.
    The human spinal cord organoid work (PMID:37643760) is the one system at the
    right axial level, and it is in vitro. The senescence study is informative
    here for a different reason: it found p19Arf mediated microcephaly but
    explicitly not exencephaly or spinal defects, which shows the two can come
    apart mechanistically within one model.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:34737154
    reference_title: NRF2 activation inhibits valproic acid-induced neural tube defects in mice.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      The prevalence of open neural tubes in embryos treated with VPA significantly
      decreased with D3T pretreatment, as did the severity of the observed defects
      evaluated by a morphological assessment.
    explanation: >-
      The rescue is scored on open neural tubes in mouse, which in this model means
      cranial defects.
  - reference: PMID:35700169
    reference_title: Aberrant induction of p19Arf-mediated cellular senescence contributes to neurodevelopmental defects.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: MODEL_ORGANISM
    snippet: >-
      Critically, through genetic and functional studies, we identified p19Arf as
      the instrumental mediator of senescence and microcephaly, but, surprisingly,
      not exencephaly and spinal defects.
    explanation: >-
      Demonstrates within one model that the brain-growth and neural-tube arms are
      mechanistically separable, which is the general form of the concern here.
- discussion_id: fvs_growth_disagreement
  kind: CONTROVERSY
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    Is prenatal valproate associated with restricted fetal growth or microcephaly?
    A Nordic cohort of 4.5 million children found no association for either, and
    the consensus statement reports no significant increase in intrauterine growth
    restriction. The AAN practice guideline advises avoiding valproate partly to
    reduce small-for-gestational-age risk.
  attaches_to:
  - phenotypes#Small for gestational age
  rationale: >-
    The disagreement may be a vintage difference rather than a true conflict: the
    guideline's systematic review closed in August 2022 and the Nordic cohort
    published in 2024. It matters beyond the phenotype list, because the mouse
    senescence mechanism curated here produces microcephaly, and if microcephaly
    is not a human feature then that branch models something the human condition
    does not show. The phenotype is curated with evidence in both directions and
    the lowest frequency band rather than being asserted or dropped.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:38476755
    reference_title: "Prenatal exposure to antiseizure medications and fetal growth: a population-based cohort study from the Nordic countries."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      We identified 4,494,918 children (males: 51.3%, 2,306,991/4,494,918),
      including 38,714 (0.9%) children of mothers with epilepsy.
    explanation: The size of the cohort finding no association, which is why it carries weight here.
- discussion_id: fvs_paternal_exposure
  kind: OPEN_QUESTION
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    Regulators have advised precaution about paternal valproate use before
    conception. Should paternal exposure be curated as an environmental factor in
    this entry?
  attaches_to:
  - environmental#Maternal valproate therapy during pregnancy
  rationale: >-
    It is not curated here. The largest study available, pooling Norwegian and
    Taiwanese national cohorts, found the crude association disappeared once
    restricted to fathers with an indication for antiseizure medication and once
    compared against active comparators — the pattern of confounding by
    indication rather than of a causal effect. Several other 2025-2026 studies
    address the same question and were not assessed here. If a paternal effect
    were established it would not belong in this entry anyway, since fetal
    valproate syndrome is defined by fetal exposure; it would be a separate
    exposure-outcome relationship.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:41733407
    reference_title: Valproate Use by Fathers and Risk of Neurodevelopmental Disorders in Children.
    supports: REFUTE
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      In the indication-restricted PS-FSW analyses, the adjusted hazard ratios of
      NDDs among offspring exposed versus unexposed to valproate among fathers with
      an antiseizure medication indication were 1.20 (95% CI, 0.75 to 1.94) in
      Norway and 1.12 (95% CI, 0.92 to 1.35) in Taiwan.
    explanation: >-
      The association does not survive restriction to fathers with the indication,
      which is the comparison that addresses confounding.
- discussion_id: fvs_episignature_and_cell_adhesion
  kind: EMERGING_HYPOTHESIS
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    The blood methylation episignature of valproate-exposed individuals is
    enriched for cell-adhesion genes and overrepresents the cadherin superfamily.
    The pathophysiology here has a cell-junction node based on exposed human
    spinal cord organoids. Are these the same biology, and if so does the
    episignature record a persistent trace of the junctional lesion?
  attaches_to:
  - pathophysiology#Loss of Neuroepithelial Cell-Junction Integrity
  rationale: >-
    Neither source makes the connection; this entry noticed that they name the
    same gene families and is recording the observation as a hypothesis rather
    than curating an edge. The two are very far apart in tissue and time — blood
    in a living person against neuroepithelium during neurulation — so a shared
    gene-set enrichment is weak evidence of a shared mechanism and could reflect
    nothing more than cadherins being large, methylation-rich gene families.
    Testing it would mean asking whether the episignature is enriched for the
    specific junctional genes the organoids change.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:39097820
    reference_title: Discovery of DNA methylation signature in the peripheral blood of individuals with history of antenatal exposure to valproic acid.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Gene set enrichment analysis demonstrated an enrichment for terms associated
      with cell adhesion, including significant overrepresentation of the cadherin
      superfamily.
    explanation: One half of the observation.
  - reference: PMID:37643760
    reference_title: Transcriptional Signature of Valproic Acid-Induced Neural Tube Defects in Human Spinal Cord Organoids.
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: IN_VITRO
    snippet: >-
      we identified and verified the significant changes in cell-cell junctional
      genes/proteins in VPA-treated organoids using transcriptomic and immunostaining
      analysis
    explanation: The other half. The connection between them is this entry's, not either source's.
- discussion_id: fvs_sensory_phenotype_unbound
  kind: CURATION_TODO
  status: OPEN
  prompt: >-
    Sensory problems were reported in 80.6% of individuals with a diagnosis of
    fetal valproate spectrum disorder, which would make them among the commonest
    features, and the rat model independently shows altered sensory thresholds.
    No HPO term is bound for this in the entry. Which term should carry it?
  attaches_to:
  - phenotypes#Autism
  rationale: >-
    The source says "sensory problems" without specifying modality or direction,
    and HPO's candidates require a commitment the source does not make.
    Binding an approximate term to the second-commonest reported feature would be
    worse than leaving it visible as a gap. The rat model's finding — lower pain
    sensitivity and higher sensitivity to non-painful stimuli — is more specific
    but is a rodent result and cannot be used to bind a human phenotype. A source
    specifying the sensory phenotype in humans would close this.
  evidence:
  - reference: PMID:37666366
    reference_title: "Neurodevelopmental outcomes in children and adults with Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder: A contribution from the ConcePTION project."
    supports: SUPPORT
    evidence_source: HUMAN_CLINICAL
    snippet: >-
      Rates of neurodevelopmental disorders, particularly autistic spectrum disorders
      (62.9%) and sensory problems (80.6%) are particularly central to the FVSD
      phenotype.
    explanation: >-
      The unbound phenotype and its frequency, recorded here so the gap is visible
      rather than silent.
notes: >-
  The question of whether this should be a Disease entry, a has_subtypes entry on an existing disease, or out of
  scope was decided as a Disease: it has its own MONDO term, its own consensus
  diagnostic criteria, a facial gestalt and malformation pattern that no parent
  disease carries, and the same standing as Fetal_Alcohol_Spectrum_Disorder,
  which this entry follows structurally.

  Three candidate upstream mechanisms are curated in parallel and deliberately
  not ranked, but they are not equally supported and the entry does not claim they
  are. The HDAC and folate branches each carry dissociation evidence of the same
  form — a structurally related analogue that lacks the teratogenic property also
  lacks the biochemical one (PMID:16485903, PMID:1574172). The redox branch has no
  analogue dissociation at all; it rests on pharmacological rescue in two
  directions and carries a REFUTE item of its own, because the same study found no
  oxidative damage to macromolecules. None of the three accounts for the whole
  phenotype. The
  fvs_mechanism_attribution_across_entries discussion records that three existing
  dismech entries describe this mechanism differently from each other and from
  this entry, and proposes a module as the fix.

  Two deliberate omissions. The 30-40% neurodevelopmental impairment figure that
  circulates in regulatory material is not curated, because it traces to EMA and
  MHRA summaries rather than to a primary source that could be quoted. Paternal
  exposure is not curated as an environmental factor; see the
  fvs_paternal_exposure discussion.

  Frequencies are not recorded for the facial features. The gestalt contributes
  to the diagnostic criteria, so a frequency measured in diagnosed cohorts is
  partly circular, and the consensus states that the features are not required
  for the diagnosis.
📚

Evaluations and curation notes (1)

Create: Fetal Valproate Syndrome · 2026-10-05T17:26:19Z · View source

Created kb/disorders/Fetal_Valproate_Syndrome.yaml for issue #13597 as a Disease entry rather than a has_subtypes entry on an existing disease: it has its own MONDO term, its own consensus diagnostic criteria, and a malformation pattern no parent disease carries. Structurally it follows Fetal_Alcohol_Spectrum_Disorder. DEEP RESEARCH. falcon was requested and is not configured here, so the run used --fallback and was produced by claude_code, recording fell_back, requested_provider and provider_attempts in its frontmatter. Two artifacts are committed with the same report body and different validation results. The run's own frontmatter (the claude_code-named file, which carries template_sha) sets needs_review: true - 71 of 72 references resolved, with PMID:35639399 unresolved and flagged as possibly fabricated, and 54 of 71 assessed references scored on topic. A later re-validation (the falcon-named file) resolved all 72, because PMID:35639399 resolves on retry; it is real (Bjork 2022, JAMA Neurology), was fetched successfully, and is now cited in the entry, so that flag was a transport failure and not a fabrication. Both runs flagged 15 to 17 references as off topic; that score is computed against the report's own vocabulary and was not treated as a verdict. Duplicate preflight ran against origin/main, all PRs and all issues: no existing entry, no prior PR, only issue #13597. just check-genereviews --online with 7 synonyms present returned NO_CHAPTER, so no GeneReviews baseline exists. CONTENT. A 16-node pathophysiology chain with one root (the exposure) and three terminal outcomes: failed neural tube closure, craniofacial dysmorphogenesis, neurodevelopmental impairment. Three upstream branches are curated in parallel and deliberately not ranked - class I HDAC inhibition, folate receptor antagonism, embryonic redox disruption - but the entry states they are not equally supported: the first two carry analogue-dissociation evidence, the redox branch rests on pharmacological rescue and carries a REFUTE item of its own. 36 phenotypes, 6 treatments, 4 animal and 5 experimental models including the Valone 2026 83-donor human neural progenitor panel (PMID:41935183) named in the issue, and 7 discussions. Contradictions carried rather than resolved: the progenitor proliferation/differentiation direction conflicts between human in vitro and mouse in vivo; folic acid protects across antiseizure medications but not against valproate-associated spina bifida; small-for-gestational-age has evidence both ways and no frequency. A discussion records that Anencephaly, Spina_Bifida_Cystica and Autism_Spectrum_Disorder describe this mechanism differently from each other and from this entry, and proposes a shared module. Not curated, each recorded as a visible gap: the 30-40% neurodevelopmental figure (traces only to regulatory summaries with no quotable primary source); paternal exposure (association does not survive restriction to fathers with an indication, PMID:41733407); the 80.6% sensory phenotype (no HPO term the source's wording supports); laryngomalacia, epicanthic folds and the non-myopia ophthalmological anomalies (named in the consensus chapter's tables, which the cached abstract-level extraction does not contain). Two treatment_term bindings are constrained by the schema: the TreatmentActionTerm enum admits only NCIT descendants of NCIT:C25218, so MAXO:0001015 and MAXO:0001128, which name the two actions exactly, were replaced with NCIT:C15843 and NCIT:C64894 and the mismatch noted in both treatments. Fetal Valproate Syndrome (Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder): Research Report
claude-fable-5-1, claude-haiku-4-5-20251001 72 citations 2026-10-05T12:48:52.524098

Fetal Valproate Syndrome (Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder): Research Report

Target: Fetal Valproate Syndrome, MONDO:0012275. Category: Environmental (teratogenic embryofetopathy). Compiled: 2026-10-05.

How to read this report

  • Quote reliability. Quotes come from PubMed/Europe PMC abstracts fetched through a summarising tool, so treat every quote as a lead until it is re-checked against references_cache/ (just fetch-reference PMID:<id>).
  • [V] marks quotes from abstracts returned in full.
  • [F] marks fragments, which are more likely to contain small wording errors.
  • Only PMID:23613074 was read directly from the local reference cache.
  • Ontology terms. Terms marked ✓ were read from this repository's cache/*/terms.csv in this session (CURIE and label confirmed). Terms listed "by label only" were not looked up and have no CURIE on purpose.
  • Sources not retrieved.
  • The Orphanet page (ORPHA:1906) was blocked, and no ORPHA_1906.md exists in the local cache.
  • The abstracts for DiLiberti 1984 (PMID:6439041) and Clayton-Smith 1995 (PMID:8544193) were not returned.
  • The Clayton-Smith 2019 consensus full text was read only as a tool summary, so its figures need checking against the paper.

1. Disease information

Overview. Fetal valproate syndrome (FVS) is a drug-induced embryofetopathy caused by in utero exposure to valproic acid/sodium valproate (VPA). It has three components: a recognisable facial gestalt, major and minor congenital malformations, and neurodevelopmental impairment.

  • MONDO defines it as "an anticonvulsant drug-related embryofetopathy that can occur when a fetus is exposed to valproic acid (VPA), characterized by distinct facial dysmorphism, congenital anomalies and developmental delay (especially in language and communication)" (via OLS).
  • A 2019 European Reference Network (ERN-ITHACA) consensus proposed the name Fetal Valproate Spectrum Disorder (FVSD): "we feel this better encompasses the broad range of effects seen following VPA exposure in utero" [F] (PMID:31324220).
  • The same abstract describes the pattern as "major and minor congenital anomalies, facial dysmorphic features, and neurodevelopmental difficulties, including cognitive and social impairments" [F].

Identifiers (from the MONDO:0012275 record via OLS unless noted):

Resource Identifier
MONDO MONDO:0012275 "fetal valproate syndrome"
OMIM 609442 (MONDO synonym "susceptibility to valproate embryopathy"; also cited as "fetal valproate syndrome (OMIM #609442)" in PMID:39097820)
Orphanet ORPHA:1906
MeSH C536525 (supplementary concept)
UMLS C0236026

GARD 5447
MedDRA 10016524
ICD-10 Not in the MONDO record; Q86.8 ("other congenital malformation syndromes due to known exogenous causes") is from memory and unverified

Synonyms. FVS; fetal valproic acid syndrome; valproic acid embryopathy; valproate embryopathy; susceptibility to valproate embryopathy; fetal valproate spectrum disorder (FVSD). The condition is also a subset of "fetal anticonvulsant syndrome" (FACS).

Data provenance. Knowledge is aggregated at disease level. The main sources are: - prospective pregnancy registries (EURAP, the North American AED Pregnancy Registry, the UK Epilepsy and Pregnancy Register); - national health-register cohorts (Denmark, the Nordic SCAN-AED study, US claims data); - EUROCAT congenital-anomaly registries; - clinic-ascertained case series and case reports.

No EHR-derived individual patient data were used here.

2. Etiology

Causal factor

Maternal use of valproate during pregnancy is the single necessary cause. The disease is environmental and not Mendelian.

Risk factors (modifiers of risk given exposure)

Dose is the strongest and best-documented modifier. - EURAP: major congenital malformations (MCM) in "142 (10·3%) of 1381 pregnancies" on valproate monotherapy [F], with risk rising with dose (p<0.0001 as reported by the tool). Even ≤650 mg/day carried higher risk than levetiracetam (OR 2.43, 95% CI 1.30–4.55, tool-reported) (PMID:29680205). - Cochrane 2023: "Multiple studies found that the MCM risk is dose-dependent." [F] (PMID:37647086). - Cochrane 2014, cognition: "A dose effect for VPA was reported in six studies, with higher doses (800 to 1000 mg daily or above) associated with a poorer cognitive outcome in the child." [F] (PMID:25354543). - The consensus statement gives an overall malformation risk of about 11%, rising to about 24% above 1500 mg/day (tool summary of PMID:31324220). - A narrative review states doses below 600 mg have limited teratogenic potential (tool paraphrase of PMID:38203562). EURAP shows there is no risk-free dose.

Timing. - First-trimester exposure drives structural malformations (PMID:20558369). - Exposure in the second half of pregnancy still carries autism risk: exposure was defined "from gestational week 19 until delivery" in the US cohort, where valproate remained associated (PMID:38507750) [V].

Polytherapy. Combined use with other antiseizure medications raises malformation rates (tool paraphrase of PMID:38203562).

Previous affected child. - UK register: "For women whose first child had a congenital malformation there was a 16.8% risk of having another child with a congenital malformation, compared with 9.8% for women whose first child did not have a malformation (relative risk 1.73, 95% confidence interval [CI] 1.01-2.96)." [V] (PMID:23167802). - The valproate-specific recurrence was 21.9% (RR 1.47, 95% CI 0.68–3.20), a trend that was not significant. - Sibling clusters are reported in PMID:12196666, PMID:11223853 and PMID:15669094.

Genetic susceptibility: maternal folate-pathway genotype. Human evidence is suggestive and inconsistent. - Dean 2007: "No effect of the child's genotype on congenital malformation, neurodevelopmental disorder or FACS was detected." [F] Mothers homozygous for MTHFR 677TT had a three- to four-fold higher risk of an affected child than 677CC mothers (PMID:17853476; earlier report PMID:10563481). - Kini 2007: maternal MTHFR genotype was associated with malformations, but "the main teratogenic effects are mediated through other mechanisms" [F] (PMID:17951123). - Mouse model: VPA "significantly increased the malformation rate of CT and TT heterozygote and homozygote mice, respectively, compared to that of CC mice (P < 0.05)" [V] (PMID:40645457; MODEL_ORGANISM).

Paternal exposure is not established as a cause. A Norway/Taiwan cohort concluded: "Paternal use of valproate within the 90 days before conception was not associated with an increased risk of NDDs in offspring after adjustment for confounding" [V] (PMID:41733407). The earlier regulatory precaution (EMA/MHRA, 2024) rested on an unpublished registry signal and was not retrieved here.

Maternal epilepsy itself confounds some outcomes. In Denmark, the ASD hazard ratio fell from 2.9 to 1.7 (95% CI 0.9–3.2) when the cohort was restricted to mothers with epilepsy, though childhood autism remained elevated (HR 2.9, 95% CI 1.4–6.0) (PMID:23613074) [V, local cache].

Protective factors

Periconceptional folic acid. The evidence is partial. - NEAD: higher periconceptional folate use correlated with better cognitive outcomes (tool paraphrase of PMID:23352199). - Nordic target-trial emulation (all antiseizure medications): "Initiation of high-dose folic acid in the 12 weeks before pregnancy was associated with a reduced risk of MCA in offspring of women using ASMs while initiation in the 12 weeks after pregnancy onset was not." [V] (RR 0.55, 95% CI 0.25–0.91; PMID:41876129). This result is not valproate-specific. - AAN/AES/SMFM guideline: "Clinicians should prescribe at least 0.4 mg of folic acid supplementation daily preconceptionally and during pregnancy to any PWECP treated with an ASM" [V] (PMID:38748979). - Folate does not abolish valproate teratogenicity.

Substitution or dose reduction before conception is the only reliable prevention (see section 13).

No protective genetic variants have been identified in humans.

Gene–environment interaction

The working model is exposure × maternal one-carbon-metabolism genotype (MTHFR 677C>T), supported by the mouse knock-in model (PMID:40645457) and by familial recurrence (PMID:23167802, PMID:12196666). Placental drug transporter variation has been proposed as a further modifier (PMID:17597651, review).

3. Phenotypes

Frequencies come from mixed ascertainment. Registry figures are prospective and unbiased. Clinic or support-group series (PMID:10882750, PMID:37666366) over-represent severe cases.

Craniofacial dysmorphism

Onset is congenital and features are most recognisable in infancy and early childhood. The consensus list (tool summary of PMID:31324220) is: "flat philtrum, thin upper lip, full, everted lower lip, short anteverted nose, small mouth, epicanthic folds, neat arched eyebrows, broad nasal root", plus metopic ridging. In Aberdeen, "52% of exposed children had facial dysmorphism compared with 25% of those not exposed" (all antiepileptics; PMID:11950853) [V].

Feature HPO (✓ = cache-verified)
Epicanthic folds HP:0000286 Epicanthus ✓

Flat philtrum HP:0000319 Smooth philtrum ✓

Broad nasal root/bridge HP:0000431 Wide nasal bridge ✓
Small mouth HP:0000160 Narrow mouth ✓

Infraorbital groove, medial eyebrow deficiency by label only (not in cache)

Major congenital malformations

Overall MCM prevalence after monotherapy: - 10.3% (EURAP, PMID:29680205); - 9.9% (95% CI 8.5–11.5; EURAP update, PMID:38497990); - 9.2% (31/337; North American registry, PMID:40669027); - pooled "9.8% (95% CI 8.1 to 11.9) from cohort data" [F] (PMID:37647086).

Specific malformations, from the EUROCAT case-control study of first-trimester monotherapy versus no antiepileptic (PMID:20558369) [F, numbers as returned]:

Malformation Odds ratio (95% CI) HPO
